##### 2. What changes because of the boost SA rulings (T-2, R-5)

The boost SA review (2026-09-30, re-validated 2026-10-01) ruled how credits are **added** for both products, and asked this session to acknowledge it (boost RV-6, R-5). **BA acknowledges it here, on behalf of the credit deduction requirement; SA confirms (S11-SQ-1):**

1. **No separate grants table.** Admin grants and boost purchases share **one** append-only table, `business_os_credit_lots`, with a `source` of `'admin_grant'` or `'boost_purchase'`, written **only** through one `SECURITY INVOKER` function, `business_os_record_credit_lot(...)`, with a unique idempotency key. The slice 11 table's "A grants table carrying actor, reason, `created_at` and `expires_at` (SA-S8)" is **superseded** (see Fragment B's annotation). SA-S8's *standards* (FK to `auth.users`, owner-SELECT-only RLS, repository-only access, `SECURITY INVOKER`, registries, actor and reason stored on the row, audit flushed) still apply — to the lots table.
2. **Reduce and end are append-only.** A reduction or an early end is a **reversal row against a lot**, never an UPDATE of the lot.
3. **Lots never touch the charge path.** No lot or reversal touches `business_os_credit_totals` or `business_os_record_credit_charge`; the period totals stay pure consumption, so a period reset cannot affect a lot (C7 unchanged by construction).
4. **Two figures for the owner.** The card keeps **"plan left"** and **"extra credits"** as two figures. The combined remaining is for enforcement (slice 9), never displayed as one number (R-5 (c), boost FR-24, T-13).
5. **`granted` means the lot balance, not lifetime grants.** In `computeCreditBalance`, `granted` is the lots' credits minus reversals minus draws recorded in **earlier** periods (R-5 (b)). See S11-SQ-4: a lot created during the current period must count at once, so "at the start of the period" cannot be read literally.
6. **Ordering flips.** Because boost is not built, **slice 11 is now the first to create `business_os_credit_lots`** (and its reversal table). Boost slice 2 must not create it again; it adds only its purchase and cap-override tables and functions (Fragment G).

##### 3. What the admin and the owner get, in plain language

**The admin (Barak or the second admin):**
- On a business's entry under **Businesses**, a **Credits** block: this period's plan allowance, what has been used, the extra credits on the account, and what is left in total — plus **which rule set the allowance** (the plan, the cohort or an override). No SQL.
- Under it, the list of extra-credit grants: how many credits, how many are left, when each expires, why it was given, who gave it, when.
- A **Give credits** action: amount, reason, and an end date or "no end date" (S11-D-2). A **Take back** action on one grant: how many, and why. Both are refused on the admin's own account.
- Every change leaves an audit entry with who, which account, before and after, and the reason.

**The owner:**
- Nothing changes until sub-slice 11d. Then the dashboard card shows a second, separate figure — for example **"Extra credits: 200"** — under the plan figure, with one line saying extra credits are used after the plan's credits and do not reset monthly (S11-D-1).
- The owner never sees the admin's reason or name (S11-D-4).
- **Important, and true today:** nothing is enforced (shadow). A grant does not unblock anybody, because nobody is blocked. It becomes meaningful when slice 9 counts it and enforcement is switched on (S11-KI-1).

##### 4. Proposed split, sizes, scope and guardrails

The slice as written (lots table, grant / reduce / end, the per-account view, the owner figure, plus the old "count grants in `balance.ts`") is about 6.5–7 days. That is over the comfort line, so BA proposes four sub-slices, each its own PR. Counting grants in `balance.ts` moves to **slice 9**, which is where `balance.ts` becomes real (FR-17's grant half, AC-13's grant half).

| Sub-slice | Scope title | Contents | Guardrails (must not) | Depends on | Size |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **11a** | **Credit lots table, and the prod runbook** | Migration **20261017** (S11-SQ-8): `business_os_credit_lots` per T-2 with `source` CHECK covering both `'admin_grant'` and `'boost_purchase'` from day one (S11-SQ-1); the reversal table (S11-SQ-2); `business_os_record_credit_lot(...)` and the reversal function, `SECURITY INVOKER`, EXECUTE to `service_role` only, `search_path` pinned; RLS on, owner column-level SELECT that hides reason, actor and idempotency key; `service_role` SELECT and INSERT only. `BusinessOsCreditLotRepository` (`new-repository` skill: client injection, `RepositoryResult`, `.eq('user_id', …)`). Registries: `businessOwnedTables.ts`, `purge/descriptors.ts` (never purged), `accountDeletionPolicy.ts` (minimise; user id `ON DELETE SET NULL` as charges, `20261015…sql:28`). **Runbook in the slice 3 §6.4.1 style:** a read-only checker SQL (one grid, row 0 the verdict, including a lot-balance rebuild parity row like C7), an **always-rollback write probe**, a rollback script, no `--` comments (the `entitlementSqlScripts.guard` rule). The user applies it to PROD by hand | Nothing wired, no route, no UI. `business_os_credit_charges`, `_totals` and `business_os_record_credit_charge` byte-identical; C7 still passes. No UPDATE or DELETE grant on any new table. No boost purchase table | Slice 3 (merged) | ~2–2.5 d |
| **11b** | **Admin give / take back credits (API)** | Two (or three) new ops on the **existing** admin path (S11-SQ-5): give credits (amount, reason, expiry key per S11-D-2, idempotency per S11-SQ-1); take back from one lot (amount or "the rest", reason). **Self-account guard** (S11-SQ-6). Pre-checks with explicit codes, never a database error: not a Business OS tenant (404), no plan row (409), held invitee (409, S11-D-3), lot not on this account (404), lot already ended or expired (409), more than is left (409, S11-D-6), over the typo ceiling (400, S11-D-5). Audit entry per op with actor, target, before/after lot balance, reason, flushed before the response; new `AUDIT_EVENTS` keys registered (S11-SQ-11). Integration tests: happy path, 401 / 403, 400, own account, each 404 / 409 | API-only: no button anywhere. Never `profiles.role`. Account from the URL path only, never the body. No write outside the lot functions. No change to the 7 existing ops' behaviour unless SA extends the self-account guard to them (S11-SQ-6) | 11a merged **and applied to PROD** | ~1.5 d |
| **11c** | **Admin per-account credit view** | The read side of FR-31 (S11-SQ-9): allowance (this period, or the trial's total), used (from the totals row, same period function as the card), extra credits (lot balance), combined remaining, and the deciding layer of `credits.allowance` (from the entitlements snapshot, `decidedBy`). The lot list (source, credits, left, expiry, reason, actor, created). A **Credits** block in the Businesses row panel (`BusinessOsPanel.tsx`) with the Give / Take back forms calling 11b's ops (reason required; a confirm step naming the account; plain-language refusals), per S11-D-8 | Plans page stays read-only. No change to `EntitlementSnapshot`'s existing output. No dollars beside credits in the same figure; no sum across anything but credits. No client import of `lib/business-os/entitlements/**` (or registered per the skill) | 11b merged | ~2 d |
| **11d** | **Owner sees extra credits** | The owner payload's `granted` becomes the lot balance (`ownerCreditUsage.ts:250`); the card shows it as a **second figure** with the one-line explanation, en / he / es, per S11-D-1; the payload allow-list pin is updated deliberately (boost RV-5); owner read through the owner RLS client and the column-level grant | Never a combined total on the card (R-5 (c)). Never the reason, actor or idempotency key in the owner payload. No change to the plan-left logic of slice 6a. No "top up" prompt | 11a merged and applied; may wait for slice 9 or boost slice 5 (S11-D-1, timing) | ~1 d |
| **Total** | | | | | **~6.5–7 d** |

**Why this cut.** 11a is the only part that needs the user at the database, so it goes first and alone, exactly like slice 3b-i. 11b is backend only and testable without UI. 11c is the admin surface in one place. 11d is small, owner-facing, and its right moment depends on S11-D-1. **11a and 11b may be merged into one PR** if the user prefers fewer PRs (about 4 days); BA recommends keeping them apart because the migration must be applied by hand before the ops are usable anyway.

**Guardrails for the whole slice** (on top of the slice 11 table's): no change to `business_os_credit_charges`, `_totals` or `business_os_record_credit_charge`; no draw / consumption rows (slice 9 / 10, S11-SQ-3); no purchase, payment or boost table (boost slice 2); no enforcement; no change to `balance.ts` (slice 9); no Pilot-Credit table; no new owner notification (email, in-app).

##### 5. Draft acceptance criteria (S11-AC-n)

*(Draft; final once the user decides S11-D-1 to S11-D-8 and SA rules S11-SQ-1 to S11-SQ-16.)*

- [ ] **S11-AC-1** (11a; FR-30, SA-S8, T-2) The migration creates `business_os_credit_lots` and its reversal table with RLS on, owner column-level SELECT only (reason, actor and idempotency key not readable by `authenticated`), and `service_role` SELECT and INSERT only — no UPDATE or DELETE grant to anyone. The record function is `SECURITY INVOKER`, EXECUTE to `service_role` only, `search_path` pinned, and a second call with the same idempotency key records nothing and reports so. `source` accepts `'admin_grant'` and `'boost_purchase'` and refuses anything else. The checker passes on PROD, the write probe always rolls back, and `business_os_credit_charges`, `_totals` and `business_os_record_credit_charge` are byte-identical before and after (C7 passes).
- [ ] **S11-AC-2** (11b; FR-30, AC-22) A grant requires an admin; an invalid body (amount not a positive number, missing reason, missing expiry key, an unknown key) gets 400; a valid grant writes exactly one lot with `source = 'admin_grant'`, the admin as actor, the reason and the expiry, and one audit entry with actor, target, before / after lot balance and reason, flushed before the response. Repeating the same request (same idempotency key) writes no second lot and no second audit entry.
- [ ] **S11-AC-3** (11b; FR-30, AC-22) A non-admin is refused (403), and an admin aiming a grant or a reduction at **their own account** is refused with an explicit code, writing nothing.
- [ ] **S11-AC-4** (11b; FR-30, T-2) A reduction or an end writes one reversal row and leaves the lot row unchanged; asking for more than is left is refused (409); reducing an ended or expired lot is refused (409); a lot id that belongs to another account gets 404 and writes nothing.
- [ ] **S11-AC-5** (11b; T-2) Granting and reducing change no row of `business_os_credit_totals` and no charge row, and the owner card's "plan left" is unchanged.
- [ ] **S11-AC-6** (11c; FR-31, AC-23 view half) For one account an admin reads, without SQL: the allowance and the layer that decided it, used this period, extra credits, combined remaining, and every lot with its source, credits, left, expiry, reason, actor and date. "Used" equals the Costs & credits tab's credits for the same account and period. A non-admin gets 403.
- [ ] **S11-AC-7** (11d; FR-24, R-5 (c)) The owner card shows plan left and extra credits as two figures, with no combined total; the payload carries no reason, actor, idempotency key, token, dollar or cost field (allow-list test).
- [ ] **S11-AC-8** (11a / 11c) The lot balance can be rebuilt from the append-only rows (credits granted − reversals; − draws once they exist), and the checker has a parity row that proves it.
- [ ] **S11-AC-9** (11b; S11-D-3) A grant to a trial account follows S11-D-3; a grant to an account held for payment, or with no plan row, is refused with an explicit code.
- [ ] **S11-AC-10** (11a; boost F-11 / NFR-12) The new tables are registered in `businessOwnedTables.ts`, `purge/descriptors.ts` (never purged) and `accountDeletionPolicy.ts` (minimise), and the registry guard tests pass.

**Folded in after the SA review (2026-10-02, S11-C-1).** These amend the draft ACs above; the draft text stays as written.

- **S11-CR-1:** both credit ops and the admin view route refuse the platform account with 409 `platform_account`, before the tenant check (amends S11-AC-3 and S11-AC-9).
- **S11-CR-2:** a replayed grant or reduction (same idempotency key, same account and amount) returns the original result with `replayed: true` and writes **no** audit entry; a key reused for a different account or amount is 409 `idempotency_key_conflict` (amends S11-AC-2).
- **S11-CR-3:** slice 11 shows **no combined remaining** to anyone. The admin view shows plan left, extra credits and "over the plan this period" as separate figures; the combined figure arrives with slice 9 (amends S11-AC-6).
- **S11-CR-4:** the reversal function takes a per-account `pg_advisory_xact_lock` (no row lock is possible without UPDATE); slice 9 / 10's consumption writer must use the same key.
- **S11-BQ-1 = Yes:** the own-account refusal (403 `own_account`) runs first for **all nine** ops on the route, not only the two credit ops (amends S11-AC-3; 11b +0.25 d).
- **S11-D-3 / S11-AC-9:** a held (unpaid invited) account is refused through `readPaymentHold`, failing closed; a lot never changes plan dates.
- **S11-D-5:** a single grant above 100,000 credits is refused with 400; whole credits only.
- **Sizes after SA:** 11a ≈2.5 d, 11b ≈2.25 d (with BQ-1), 11c ≈2 d, 11d ≈1 d — ≈7.75 d, four PRs. 11b does not merge until 11a is applied to PROD and the checker / probe output is pasted into the 11a workplan.

### Slice 12 — Guards before switch-on

| | |
|---|---|
| **Goal** | Close the two gaps that must be closed before enforcement can be switched on: an expired plan must stop the owner's AI, and strangers must not be able to spend an owner's credits. |
| **What the owner / user gets** | Once enforcement is on: an owner whose plan has lapsed gets a clear "your plan has ended" instead of AI; and a form-spammer flooding an owner's enquiry form cannot drain the owner's credits — past a daily cap, lead replies go out as template text. |
| **Scope (in)** | FR-32: a lapsed or expired plan stops owner-facing AI, decided by the entitlements lifecycle. SA-S7: a per-account daily cap on charged stranger-triggered (`external`) actions, like the image cap; past it, the lead reply degrades to template text. The cap counts charge rows whose stored trigger is **`external`** (SQ-15), not a list of action types, so a future stranger-triggered type is counted automatically. **To consider (added 2026-09-30, D3):** because the plan allowance is a generous ceiling rather than a limit owners are expected to hit, the **per-action caps are the practical abuse guard** — the per-business daily image cap exists today (images dominate cost: one image ≈ 250 credits) and the chat daily budget exists today; this slice considers whether a further **chat and / or image cap** is needed before switch-on. BA suggestion: decide it from the Costs & credits report once real accounts exist; the pricing doc's "any account above ~25% of its allowance" trigger is the signal. |
| **Guardrails (out / must not touch)** | Does not use or change `account_frozen` (it would freeze paying Business OS customers through the agent platform). Does not switch enforcement on (S-5, G-1). No agent-platform change. Client-facing work is never stopped, only degraded. |
| **Depends on** | Slices 9 and 10. |
| **FRs / ACs** | FR-32. AC-24, AC-32. Also SA-S7, A-15, SQ-15, **D3**. |

Selling credits (boost purchases, auto top-up) stays in S-4b. This layer only needs a grant to **exist**; selling one does not.

| **D-1** Measure | **3** and **4** | Recording the charge in shadow → 3; the operator report and the leak check → 4 |
| **D-1b** Set the credit value and derive the allowances | **5** | Unchanged |
| **D-2** Tell the owner the truth | **6** and **7** | The card → 6; the diary and range → 7 (recommended to release together) |
| **D-3** Behave at zero | **8**, **9** and **10** | Warnings → 8; the balance check and outage behaviour → 9; pause / degrade and the template fallback → 10 |
| **D-4** Operator controls | **11** and **12** | Grants and the per-account view → 11; the lapsed-plan stop and the stranger cap (SA-S7, previously "before D-3") → 12 |

### Superseded slice plan (2026-09-28)

Kept for the record; the SA Review refers to these names. **Superseded by the plan above.**

Six slices. Each ships alone and leaves the product working. **D-0 to D-2 do not depend on G-1 or on enforcement switch-on** — they measure, charge in shadow and tell the truth, which is also the evidence the credit value and the allowances need.

| Slice | Delivers | Why first / notes |
|---|---|---|
| **D-0 Rules, and a trustworthy cost** | The declared action list (audience, setup flag, chargeable, diary label, template-fallback status) as code with a guard test, **and FR-12**: unpriced models fail loudly, a conservative documented fallback exists and flags its rows, every Layer 2-selectable model has a price, image prices resolve. No behaviour change. | Under option B the cost figure **is** the charge, so a silent $0 would charge a customer nothing (A-3). This is also where the *missing* client-facing template fallbacks are found, rather than discovered at the limit. |
| **D-1 Measure** | Per-action-type cost measurement across real accounts and the operator report (FR-33). Charging runs in shadow: the ledger fills at a provisional credit value, nothing is refused, nothing owner-facing moves. | Produces the evidence for **the credit value** and for FR-37's allowances — not for a price list, which option B does not need. Answers OQ-7, OI-1 and UD-1 with numbers instead of argument. |
| **D-1b Set the credit value and derive the allowances** | Fix the credit value as configuration; replace 250 / 500 / 1,000 / 2,000 and every `PLACEHOLDER` cohort quantity with derived figures; record the derivation; update the capability `unit` and the plans doc (FR-37). | A config change, gated by D-1's measurement. Small — but it is the moment the plans stop being invented. |
| **D-2 Tell the owner the truth** | The usage card in credits against the resolved plan allowance, split owner-triggered vs automatic; **the credit diary** (FR-26) and the own-history range (FR-27); the display-only allowance retired from the owner surface. | The first owner-visible change, and the last one that is safe to make before anything can be refused. The diary ships **with** the card: under option B it is not a nice-to-have, it is the whole of the owner's predictability. |
| **D-3 Behave at zero** | The real `balance.ts`, warnings at 80/100%, the setup pre-warning, degrade-vs-pause by audience, the fail-closed/fail-open split, and the relationship to the chat daily budget — all still inert in `shadow`. | Built and reviewable before it can refuse anyone; turns on with the entitlements flag, not with its own. |
| **D-4 Operator controls** | Admin grant / reduce, the per-account position view, and the lapsed-plan stop. | Needed before enforcement is live, or the first exhausted champion is a support ticket with no remedy (D-7). |

## 13. Business decisions for the user

| # | Decision | Status | Note |
|---|---|---|---|
| **BD-1** | **What one credit is defined against** | ✅ **DECIDED 2026-09-28 — option B, credits charged at live measured cost.** See [§2](#2-the-currency-decided-bd-1) | Simpler, and cost cannot be predicted per action. Everything after the fact: no stale price to lose money on, nothing to recalculate. Accepted: the same action varies day to day, and support cannot quote in advance — answered by the diary (FR-26) and the own-history range (FR-27) |
| **BD-2** | **Does work the product does on its own — nightly insights, the daily briefing, automatic lead replies — come out of the owner's allowance?** | Agreed in principle: **yes, with its own line, and every instance in the diary** | It is real spend, and an uncounted channel is an uncapped one — but an owner must never watch a balance fall on a day they did nothing without being told why. Under option B the diary answers that per action, not just per month |
| **BD-3** | **Does a new owner's setup — onboarding, the first website and intake generation — come out of their allowance?** | Agreed in principle: **yes, against the trial's one-off total, with a warning before it runs out** (B-12) | UD-1's worry — a new owner opening their dashboard with part of month one already spent — is answered by slice 4's measured number, by FR-23's pre-action warning (slice 8), and by the diary showing exactly what setup cost |
| **BD-4** | **How much is an AI image?** (OQ-7) | ✅ **Settled by BD-1: its actual measured cost** ($0.016–$0.25, A-17) | No per-image price to decide or maintain. The existing per-business daily image cap stays as the abuse guard |
| **BD-5** | **What happens when an owner's allowance is gone** | Agreed in principle: **degrade, don't stop** (D-12) | The owner's assistant pauses; everything touching their clients keeps working on template text. Two conditions: the check **fails closed for the owner's own AI and open for client-facing work** — the opposite of Layer 2's kill switch and of the chat budget, both of which fail open — and a client-facing action may only be degraded once it has template text to fall back to. **Refined by BQ-1 (option C):** during an outage the owner's AI keeps working unless the account was last seen at zero. *(C-1: the lead reply already has its template fallback, so the second condition is met today for the one client-facing action.)* |
| **BD-6** | **What happens to existing balances and history on day one** | Agreed in principle: **start from zero** | History is in tokens, under a rule we are replacing, for a period in which nobody was told they were metered. Leave the agent platform's balances untouched; nothing is double-charged because the two never meet |
| **BD-7** | **When the owner's dashboard changes to the new number** | Agreed in principle: **in slice 6 (was D-2), before anything can be refused**, and only after slice 5 | The number on the card today is measured against an allowance nobody is held to, in a unit we are discarding; making it true is a fix, not a feature |
| **BD-8** | **How much AI cost are we willing to carry per plan per month?** | ✅ **DECIDED 2026-09-30 (user) — by D1, D2 and D3 below: the AI cost ceiling is 25% of each plan's monthly fee.** *(Superseded status: "⬜ Pending user input — asked at slice 5, once slice 4 has real numbers".)* | The one input slice 5 needs. It replaces the 500 / 2,000 the user decided as action counts (SA-S11). BA will bring the measured cost per plan and a suggested figure; the user decides. **Resolved:** Essentials $79 → $19.75 ceiling → **19,750 credits / month**; Autopilot $129 → $32.25 → **32,250 credits / month**; Founding Partner inherits Autopilot; the trial total is split out as BD-16. Measured recurring use on the 2026-09-30 benchmark was ~60 credits per account per 30 days (0.3% / 0.2% of these allowances). Numbers, data and revisit procedure: [BUSINESS_OS_CREDIT_PRICING.md](/docs/architecture/BUSINESS_OS_CREDIT_PRICING.md) |
| **D1** *(BD-8)* | **Markup on AI cost** | ✅ **DECIDED 2026-09-30 (user): 100%** | The owner pays **2×** our AI cost. The markup is **not** applied to the charge — a charge stays measured cost ÷ $0.001 — it is expressed only in the size of each plan's allowance |
| **D2** *(BD-8)* | **How much of the fee is the AI allowance** | ✅ **DECIDED 2026-09-30 (user): 50% of the SaaS fee, expressed as credit value** | At 2× markup, the **AI cost ceiling = 25% of the fee**. Formula: allowance credits = (fee × AI share ÷ (1 + markup)) ÷ credit value. Sized on AI cost alone; a later non-AI service drawing from the same pool (BD-12) is a revisit trigger |
| **D3** *(BD-8)* | **A generous ceiling or a tight limit** | ✅ **DECIDED 2026-09-30 (user): option A — a generous, margin-protecting ceiling**, not a limit owners are expected to hit | The benchmark showed the allowance could be one to two orders of magnitude smaller and still leave >3× headroom; the user chose the ceiling. The practical abuse guards are per-action caps: the daily image cap and chat daily budget today; a chat / image cap to consider in **slice 12**. Accepted: the allowance will not drive boost purchases while usage looks like the benchmark (KI-15) |
| **BD-9** | **Slice format** | ✅ **DECIDED 2026-09-28 (user)** | Slices numbered 1, 2, 3…, each titled by its scope, a few days each, with defined scope and guardrails (§12) |
| **BD-10** | **No new tracking** | ✅ **DECIDED 2026-09-28 (user)** | `runAiAction` is the only place that measures; the audit entry and the charge row come from the same in-memory call list; one new thin table only because the audit trail cannot be the bill (§3, §4) |
| **BD-11** | **When does the owner's usage card refresh?** | ✅ **DECIDED 2026-09-28 (user)** | Three triggers: (a) right after the owner's own AI action finishes, (b) when the owner returns to the tab or page, (c) a small manual refresh icon on the card, with a brief loading state. **No background or timer polling** — automatic work shows on the next refresh (KI-12). Delivered in slice 6 (FR-39, AC-33). Today the card is fetched once on page load (A-18) |
| **BD-12** | **One credit pool for every chargeable action?** | ✅ **DECIDED 2026-09-29 (user)** | Yes. Eventually every chargeable action — AI or not, e.g. a notification email the owner sends to clients, an SMS reminder — has a measured cost converted to credits, and all draw from the **same** balance. Non-AI charges follow the same procedure as AI (measured cost after it ran → credits at the credit value → one confirmed charge row → same period, totals, corrections, diary), with few exceptions: the AI "fallback priced" flag, and the action id, which today comes from `runAiAction` — a non-AI service needs its own equivalent wrapper when built. **Nothing non-AI is designed, priced or built in this layer**; the ledger must simply not block it (§2, §4, §5, FR-40, [Adding a future chargeable service](#adding-a-future-chargeable-service)) |
| **BD-13** | **A neutral ledger** | ✅ **DECIDED 2026-09-29 (user)** | The tables and function are renamed from `business_os_ai_*` to **`business_os_credit_charges`**, **`business_os_credit_totals`** and **`business_os_record_credit_charge`** — free, as none is applied to production yet. A new **`service`** column on each charge row (`ai` for everything in this layer; later e.g. `notification_email`, `sms`), an identifier format, not a fixed list (SQ-18 — format ruled by SA 2026-09-29: required on charge rows, NULL on adjustments, lowercase ASCII letter first then letters / digits / underscore, 1–64 characters, no enum or registry, permanent ids). The totals stay **one row per account per period** (single pool). Delivered in slice 3 (3b) (FR-13, FR-40b, AC-34) |
| **BD-14** | **Every chargeable action belongs to a group** | ✅ **DECIDED 2026-09-29 (user)** | The grouping id is **required** on every charge, even a group of one. Example: one click "notify my 30 clients" = one grouping id, 30 action ids (one per email), each with its own cost, `service = notification_email` (Scenario 4, future). Delivered in slice 3 (FR-13, FR-40c, AC-34) |
| **BD-15** | **Neutral owner-facing wording** | ✅ **DECIDED 2026-09-29 (user)**; final wording ⬜ **decided in slices 6 and 7** | The owner never sees "AI credits"; the label is neutral (e.g. "Credits" / "Operating credits"). The final wording is decided in slice 6 (usage card) and slice 7 (diary). The diary shows every service's actions (FR-24, FR-26, FR-40d, AC-35) |
| **—** | **Allowance naming (for slice 5)** | ✅ **Ruled by SA 2026-09-30 (SQ-17, slice 5 review): renamed `credits.allowance`, unit `'credit'`, no alias.** *(Superseded status: "⬜ **Proposed by the user 2026-09-29 — to be ruled by SA at slice 5 (SQ-17), not decided now**".)* | The plan catalogue's `ai.actions` capability should become a **general credit allowance** when allowances are set in slice 5. SA had earlier said to keep the id `ai.actions` (SA-S11, because admin overrides in the database reference it); this is recorded as a **change to be ruled by SA at slice 5**, not decided here (FR-37, slice 5, OI-7). **Ruled:** SA measured 0 override rows on `ai.actions` and ruled for the rename, superseding SA-S11's "keep the id" |
| **BD-16** | **How large is the trial's (Test Flight) one-off credit total?** *(added 2026-09-30)* | ✅ **DECIDED 2026-09-30 (user): 2,000 credits in total** — one-off, not monthly. *(Superseded status: "⬜ **Pending user input — needed for slice 5**".)* | D1–D3 derive an allowance from a fee; the trial is $0, so the formula gives **0**, and the 250 placeholder (a count of actions) cannot stay (AC-25). Setup AI counts against the trial total and using it up ends the trial (B-12, FR-18), so it must comfortably cover a real setup. **BA suggested resolution:** size it on setup, not on the fee — the 2026-09-30 benchmark's worst-case setup month was ~347 credits (one $0.25 image dominates), so a total of about **2,000 credits** (~6× that, cost exposure ≤ ~$2 per trial) gives room for a second image and normal first-week use. The alternative anchor is a 14-day pro-rata of Essentials (~9,200 credits, exposure ≤ ~$9.20 per trial), which is more generous but prices a free trial like paid use. Business trade-off: a smaller total protects against trial abuse; a larger one never ends a genuine trial on credits. The user decides; the figure and its reason go into the pricing doc's revision log. **Decided:** the BA's suggested **2,000 credits** — ≈ **$2 maximum cost per trial**; ≈ **6× the heaviest measured setup month**; a **floor of at least 1,000 is guarded by a test**, so a later edit cannot quietly shrink it to where one image ends a genuine trial. The 14-day pro-rata (~9,200) was not chosen |
| **R-7** | **What owners and invitees see on the plan's credit line until slice 6** *(added 2026-09-30)* | ✅ **DECIDED 2026-09-30 (user): option C — show the line without a number** | Options considered: **A. show as planned** (the figure, e.g. "19,750 credits a month"); **B. hide the line**; **C. show it without a number** — **chosen**. Until slice 6, owners (Settings → Plan) and invitees (the public invite page) see **"Credits (included)"** / he **"קרדיטים (כלול)"** / es **"Créditos (incluido)"**; admins see the figures. It is a **known temporary behaviour** (KI-17): the rule lives in `customerPlanView.ts` with a temporary test, and **slice 6 removes it as a required task** (slice 6 scope, AC-36). Raised in the slice 5 workplan (T6c) |

Boost R-5 (c), FR-24 and T-13 already require bought credits to be a separate figure, and gifts and bought credits sit in the same table, so folding gifts into "left" would make the card count them differently from bought credits | **A, with one figure for every kind of extra credit** (gifted now, bought later) labelled **"Extra credits"** (he "קרדיטים נוספים", es "Créditos extra"; native review). The boost card's "Bought credits" label (T-13) becomes "Extra credits" too, so there are two figures, not three. | **S11-D-2** | **Do gifted credits expire by default?** | **A.** No end date unless the admin sets one. **B.** A default end date (for example 90 days), which the admin can change. **C.** The admin must choose every time: an end date **or** "no end date". Silence is not allowed | **C.** It is the rule the admin screens already follow for plans: "every assignment must say when it ends". It costs one click and stops a forgotten gift from living forever. Bought credits have no end date in v1 (boost FR-14); that is the boost's choice and is unaffected | ✅ **Accepted by the user 2026-10-02** (BA recommendation) |
| **S11-D-3** | **Can an admin give credits to a trial (Test Flight) account, or to an invited friend still waiting to pay?** | Trial: **A.** Yes; the extra credits can stop the trial ending early because its credits ran out, but **never extend the 14 days**. **B.** No; move them to a plan instead. Held friend: **A.** No. **B.** Yes | **Trial: A.** It mirrors the boost rule for bought credits (boost FR-19) and is the natural remedy for a trial that ran out during setup. **Held friend: A (no).** Same as the boost rule R-1: they have no access yet, so credits would do nothing. Accounts with no plan at all are refused too (the existing "no plan row" rule) | ✅ **Accepted by the user 2026-10-02** (BA recommendation) |
| **S11-D-4** | **Does the owner see why credits were given?** | **A.** Never. The reason is internal, as override reasons are today. The owner sees a fixed label such as "Added by our team". **B.** Shown as written. **C.** A separate, optional note for the owner, typed by the admin in addition to the internal reason | **A.** The reason is written for the audit trail ("unhappy after outage 12 Oct") and should be free to say so. If an owner-facing message is ever wanted, it is C, added later. No email or notification is sent either way | ✅ **Accepted by the user 2026-10-02** (BA recommendation) |
| **S11-D-5** | **What is the largest gift one admin can make alone?** | **A.** No limit. A reason and an audit entry are enough. **B.** No second admin, but a hard ceiling per gift against typos (a gift above it is refused; a bigger one is made as two gifts, deliberately). **C.** Above a threshold, a second admin must approve | **B, with a ceiling of 100,000 credits per gift** (about 3× an Autopilot month; at most about $100 of AI cost). There are two admins, both owners, so a second approval adds friction without protection (the same view as cleanup OQ-6). But "200" typed as "200000" should be impossible | ✅ **Accepted by the user 2026-10-02** (BA recommendation) |
| **S11-D-6** | **Taking back more than is left of a gift** | **A.** Stop at what is left. A gift can be taken back to zero, never below. **B.** Allow below zero (the owner owes credits) | **A.** Credits already used cannot be un-used, and a negative gift would silently eat the next month's plan. **What it means today:** nothing uses gifted credits until slice 9 is built, so "what is left" is the gift minus earlier take-backs; after slice 9 it also excludes what was used | ✅ **Ruled TECHNICAL by SA 2026-10-02: A** (stop at what is left); the user accepted the recommendations the same day |
| **S11-D-7** | **Can an admin take back bought credits too?** (only matters once boost ships) | **A.** Yes, with the same take-back action, a reason, and a stronger confirmation saying these credits were paid for. **B.** No; bought credits only change through a refund policy (parked in the boost requirement, Q11) | **A.** The boost review already plans for it: a manual clawback after a refund or dispute "is an admin reduce (slice 11)" (boost T-9). The admin view shows each lot's source, so it is never done by mistake | ✅ **Accepted by the user 2026-10-02** (BA recommendation); until boost ships there are no bought lots, and the stronger confirmation is built with the first boost lot |
| **S11-D-8** | **Where does the admin give and take back credits?** | **A.** API only for now, as plan overrides are today. **B.** A **Credits** block on the business's entry under **Businesses** (`/admin/users`), with Give / Take back buttons. **C.** On the Plans page account lookup | **B.** It is where an admin goes when a business complains (cleanup D-2), and it avoids opening the Plans page to writes before the cleanup work decides that (cleanup slice 6). It also delivers the "allowance and balance" half of cleanup sub-slice 5e, so the cleanup requirement can point at it instead of building it again; 5e keeps only the diary link | ✅ **Accepted by the user 2026-10-02** (BA recommendation) |
| **S11-BQ-1** | **Should an admin also be blocked from changing their own account's plan, dates and exceptions (the 7 existing admin ops), not only from giving themselves credits?** *(raised by SA, 2026-10-02)* | **Yes**: the own-account refusal covers all nine ops. **No**: only the two credit ops | **Yes** (SA). A self-granted plan exception is a self-grant by another name; about 0.25 d in 11b. Trade-off: an admin's own login can then only be changed by the other admin | ✅ **Accepted by the user 2026-10-02: Yes** |
