**Recorded 2026-10-02:** the user answered the slice 11 decisions with "ok" — read as accepting every BA / SA recommendation above, including S11-BQ-1 = Yes. S11-D-4 = A (fixed label "Added by our team"; no separate owner note), so the 11a migration needs no owner-note column.

## 14. Open questions for SA

- [x] **SQ-1 — Where the ledger lives, and what a row holds.** A new Business OS table, one row per charged action with a unique grouping id, **the credits charged, the measured cost, the credit-value version and the fallback flag** — versus deriving totals from `token_usage` at read time. *BA leaning: a real table.* `token_usage` counts calls, not actions, holds no credits, and cannot express a grant, a period or a reconciliation. The diary (FR-26) also needs a per-action row, not a per-call one. **SA ruling (TECHNICAL):** a real Business OS table, as the BA leans, plus a per-account per-period totals row kept in step in the same transaction. Keyed by a server-minted action id, not the grouping id (SA-B1). Details: SA Review, SQ table. *(Row contents revisited by SQ-15.)*
- [x] **SQ-2 — Idempotency and retries.** Is a unique constraint on the grouping id the whole of FR-9, given that one action legitimately produces several calls and a retry may reuse the grouping id? SA must say what happens when a retry adds calls under an existing id: update the row's total, or append an adjustment row. **SA ruling (TECHNICAL):** no. The grouping id is not one-per-action (onboarding reuses one per conversation; chat takes it from a request header). The idempotency key is an action id minted inside `runAiAction`, one per invocation; the grouping id is stored, indexed, not unique. A retry is a new invocation and a new row; no row is ever updated. See SA-B1.
- [x] **SQ-3 — The write path.** Write the ledger row inside the existing usage scope (non-blocking, confirmed) versus a claim-and-drain job on the §8.1 pattern. *BA leaning: a direct write* — the data is already in hand at the end of the action, and a queue adds a second way to lose a charge. FR-16 then means a failed write is an uncharged action; SA to rule on whether that is acceptable or needs an outbox. **Option B raises the stakes:** an uncharged action is money spent that no balance reflects. **SA ruling (TECHNICAL):** direct write at the end of `runAiAction`, awaited with a short time budget, never throwing. No outbox and no §8.1 queue. Lost writes are found by a ledger-vs-`token_usage` reconciliation in the D-1 report (SA-S9) *(now slice 4)*; a backfill is decided on the measured loss rate.
- [x] **SQ-4 — Reserve-then-settle, or check-then-charge?** *BA leaning: check before, charge after,* accepting a small bounded overspend under a burst. ⚠️ **Under option B the price is not knowable in advance** — a reservation would have to hold a guessed amount and true it up, which is the very machinery the decision avoided. Any SA ruling must account for that: a pre-check can only ask "is there anything left?", not "is there enough for this?". **SA ruling (TECHNICAL):** check-then-charge, no reservation. The pre-check asks only "is anything left?"; overspend is bounded by in-flight actions, recorded, drawn from grants if any, and never carried into the next period.
- [x] **SQ-5 — Cache freshness.** `EntitlementService` caches raw inputs for 30 s. Is a balance resolved through that cache acceptable, or does it need its own read? **SA ruling (TECHNICAL):** already decided by entitlements T-5: metered counters are never cached. The allowance may come from the 30 s snapshot; consumption is read fresh from the period totals row.
- [x] **SQ-6 — The period boundary** (T-6, still undecided): calendar month versus billing anniversary. It decides when a warning resets, when an exhausted account recovers, and what window the diary defaults to. **SA ruling (TECHNICAL, already decided):** not undecided. Entitlements T-6 (SA, 2026-09-19) chose anniversary periods from a per-account `period_anchor`, which exists in code (`account.ts`, `BusinessOsAccountPlanRepository`) and in migration `20261005` (unverified against live). The diary defaults to the current period. Not reopened.
- [x] **SQ-7 — Where the credit value lives, and who owns it.** `system_settings_config` (operator-changeable through a script, beside the Layer 2 area rows), or the entitlements config (engineering-owned, shipped in a release)? *BA leaning: the entitlements config* — it must be versioned with the allowances derived from it (FR-3, FR-37), and a change silently re-prices the whole product. **SA ruling (TECHNICAL):** the entitlements config, versioned with the matrix. Not `system_settings_config`: an operator-editable value that re-prices the whole product is the wrong kind of setting.
- [x] **SQ-8 — Rounding precision and the reconciliation rule** (FR-6). What precision does the ledger store, and how is "the sum of the rounded lines" reconciled with "the rounded sum" so the diary never appears to disagree with the balance? *BA leaning: store at the precision of the cheapest action, display whole credits, and take the balance from the unrounded sum with a documented note.* **SA ruling (TECHNICAL):** store cost from the raw per-call figures (not the audit's micro-dollar-rounded one) at 10 decimal places and credits at 6; round only for display; totals come from unrounded sums, with a one-line footnote on the diary. See SA Review.
- [x] **SQ-9 — The conservative fallback price** (FR-12b). What is "conservative" — the most expensive model in the same family, a fixed per-1k ceiling, or something else — and who signs off the number? **SA ruling (TECHNICAL):** conservative = the highest rate in the in-code price table for the same provider and kind (text, embedding, image), computed from the table rather than typed by hand. SA signs it off at code review. Once D-0 lands *(now slice 2)*, only a defect can reach this path.
- [x] **SQ-10 — A discrepancy to settle before TK-6 is scoped.** The tier-billing plan records F-28 as a live B-8 leak: "`app/api/business-os/usage/route.ts` already reads `user_subscriptions`". At `main` 7ba40a56 it **does not** — the only mention is a comment explaining why it deliberately does not (`route.ts:214-223`), and `usageSummary.ts` reads only `token_usage` and `tokens_per_pilot_credit`. Either F-28 is already closed, or it meant the agent-platform **rate** keys (`pilot_credit_cost_usd`, `tokens_per_pilot_credit`) that the route does read — which FR-36 removes anyway. SA to confirm which. **SA ruling (TECHNICAL):** F-28 was a false positive. `git log -S` shows the string `user_subscriptions` entered `usage/route.ts` only as the explanatory comment (ea35c79d) and was never a query. The route reads the agent-platform rate keys, which FR-36 retires in D-2 *(now slice 6)*. TK-6 should be re-scoped and the tier-billing plan corrected.
- [x] **SQ-11 — Sequencing.** The entitlements plan puts S-3 metering after S-5 switch-on, which is hard-gated on G-1. This requirement proposes D-0 to D-2 ship before that, in `shadow`. Does SA accept the reordering, given that charging in shadow refuses nobody? **SA ruling (TECHNICAL):** reordering accepted, with conditions: D-1's ledger write is proven unable to throw or fail an action; D-2 is hard-gated on D-1b; in `shadow` the 80/100% warnings are recorded, not delivered to owners, until D-1b ships. *(In the new numbering: slice 3's write is proven safe; slice 6 is hard-gated on slice 5; slice 8's warnings are recorded, not sent, in `shadow`. The reordering now covers slices 1–12, all in `shadow`.)*
- [x] **SQ-12 — The scope of FR-12, and `atLimit: 'by_call_site_audience'`.** Is "every model selectable in Layer 2's area settings has a price entry, enforced by a test" the right boundary, or must the whole `ai_model_pricing` / `FALLBACK_PRICING` path be reworked (cache warming, refresh, cached-input rates) before a charge may be derived from it? And is the declared action list (FR-4) the right home for the audience map — is the 16-entry `AiActionType` union the FR-30 inventory, or must that stay per call site? **SA ruling (TECHNICAL):** no full pricing rework. The boundary is SA-S2 and SA-S3: test the code defaults, cite Layer 2's existing unpriced-model gate, and signal "unpriced" to the Business OS resolver rather than changing shared pricing return values. The declared list keyed by `AiActionType` is the right home for audience, setup, charge, label and fallback. That supersedes WC-18's per-call-name inventory for these attributes; `BOS_LLM_CALLS` stays the pricing and attribution inventory.

**New questions from the 2026-09-28 reorganisation** (each with a BA-suggested resolution):

- [x] **SQ-13 — "Right at source" versus SA-S3** (raised by: BA | status: ruled 2026-09-28 | blocks: slice 2 workplan). The user wants the cost fixed at its source so `token_usage`, the audit entry and the charge all agree. SA-S3 says the shared pricing functions must not change what they return, because the agent platform uses them. How does an unpriced Business OS call show up correctly in `token_usage` without changing the shared return values? *BA suggested resolution:* the two do not conflict in practice. Slice 2's price-coverage tests make every code default priced, and Layer 2 already refuses unpriced DB-selected models (DEC-7), so an unpriced call is reachable only through a defect. For that residual case, `token_usage` keeps what we priced but the call carries the price-source signal and is logged at `error`, so it is never **silent**; the conservative price is applied by the Business OS charge resolver. SA to confirm, or to name a way to record the conservative figure in `token_usage` for Business OS calls only. **SA ruling (TECHNICAL):** **confirmed as the BA suggests; SA-S3 stands.** "At source" means the **per-call record** (`UsageCallRecord`, built in the provider) carries the true measurement plus a price-source signal; it does **not** mean `token_usage` carries a Business OS-only figure. Do **not** write the conservative figure into `token_usage`: the write is the shared `aiAnalytics.trackAICall` path used by the agent platform (`baseProvider.ts:124-160`), a product-conditional there is exactly the plugin-/product-specific branch the shared layer must not carry, and `token_usage`'s job (§4) is the provider-invoice check, which an invented figure would break. So the three records agree on every priced call, and on the residual unpriced call (reachable only through a defect after DEC-7 and slice 2's coverage tests) they **disagree visibly**: `token_usage` and the audit entry say what we measured ($0 plus an `error` log), and only the charge row carries the conservative figure with its flag. Three constraints for the slice 2 workplan: (1) the signal is derived **without changing any return value** — a companion read-only lookup over the same table (`pricing.ts:255-269` already knows "no pricing found"), not a new return shape on `calculateCostSync` / `calculateCost` / `resolveImagePrice`; (2) an **absent** signal (any provider other than `openaiProvider`, since `anthropicProvider` / `kimiProvider` are not touched) must **not** be treated as unpriced wholesale — the Business OS resolver re-checks priceability with the same lookup and falls back only when the model is genuinely unpriced, otherwise an operator who points an area at Anthropic (Layer 2 allows it) would be over-charged the class maximum on every call; this refines SA D-0 item 4; (3) slice 4's leak check must treat a fallback-flagged row (charge > `token_usage`) as reconciled-pending, not as a leak. *(BA, 2026-09-28: folded into FR-12h and slices 2 and 4.)*
- [x] **SQ-14 — Storing the unrounded cost in the audit entry** (raised by: BA | status: ruled 2026-09-28 | blocks: slice 2 workplan). SA's D-0 scoping said "no change to the audit `details` schema"; the user now wants the audit entry to stop rounding in slice 2. *BA suggested resolution:* treat it as a precision change to an existing field, not a new shape, so no schema bump in slice 2; slice 3 then bumps to `schema: 2` once, when it adds the action id. If SA judges the precision change to be a schema change, move it into slice 3's single bump instead — the requirement only needs both done before slice 3 records a charge. **SA ruling (TECHNICAL):** **confirmed — a precision change to an existing field, stays `schema: 1`, ships in slice 2.** The field name and type (`estimatedCostUsd: number`, `aiActionAudit.ts:115`) do not change, no reader parses a fixed precision, and the only reader in code is the admin audit-trail page (`app/admin/audit-trail/page.tsx:139,181`); nothing in SQL reads `details->'estimatedCostUsd'`. My D-0 "no audit `details` schema change" meant no new or renamed field; this is neither. Conditions: (1) **do not store the raw float** — the rounding exists to kill float noise (`0.30000000000000004`, comment at `:198`); round to **10 decimal places** instead of 6, matching SQ-8's `numeric(…,10)` for `cost_usd`, which keeps a ~2e-7 embedding non-zero and the noise out; (2) the tests that pin the old rounding change with it (`__tests__/aiActionAudit.test.ts:167-168` asserts the value *is* micro-rounded; `:403` still passes at 10 dp); (3) the admin page formats with `toFixed(6)`, so a 2e-7 entry would still **display** `$0.000000` — AC-29 is verified on the stored value, and SA **permits** (recommends) a one-line precision-aware formatter on that admin page inside slice 2 as a named exception to its "no UI" guardrail (admin-only, no owner surface); (4) entries written before the change stay micro-rounded — no backfill. The `schema: 2` bump stays in slice 3, once, for the action id. *(BA, 2026-09-28: folded into FR-12h, AC-29 and slice 2.)*
- [x] **SQ-15 — The thin charge row** (raised by: BA | status: ruled 2026-09-28 | blocks: slice 3 workplan). The user has decided a thin row (§4, FR-13): action id, account, time, credits, cost, credit-value version, fallback flag, action type, owner / automatic, succeeded / failed. SA's SQ-1 ruling listed a wider row (grouping id, area, the full trigger, tokens, `period_start`). *BA suggested resolution:* area comes from the action type (slice 1 list); the grouping id, the full trigger and tokens are reachable through the action id in the audit entry and `token_usage`; so none is needed on the bill. `period_start` is bookkeeping rather than tracking: if SA needs it on the row to index or rebuild the period totals, it may stay as a derived column. SA to confirm the thin row, with `period_start` as the only possible addition. **SA ruling (TECHNICAL):** **the thin row is confirmed, with three identifier-level changes and no tracking content** — this narrows my SQ-1 row (area and tokens are dropped, as the BA argues: area derives from the action type through the slice 1 list, tokens stay in `token_usage` and the audit entry). (1) **`period_start` is required, not optional**, written at charge time. The totals row is keyed `(user_id, period_start)` and must be rebuildable from the rows (FR-13); recomputing the period from `created_at` would use *today's* `period_anchor` (T-6), so a later anchor change (plan change, Stripe cycle alignment) would silently move old charges into the wrong period. (2) **Keep `group_id`** (indexed, **not** unique — SA-B1). The §4 / Scenario 1 claim that `token_usage` joins "by the action id" is **incorrect as built**: `token_usage` rows carry the grouping id as `session_id` (`usageScope.ts:45-46`) and never the action id, and adding it would be the new tracking the user ruled out. Without `group_id` on the charge row, the only path from a charge to its `token_usage` rows runs through the audit entry, which is lossy (§3 (a)) and archived (§3 (d)) — so a lost audit entry would orphan the charge from its cost detail, and slice 4's leak-check drill-down (SA-S9, "by `session_id`") could not name the leaking group. BA to correct §4 and Scenario 1 step 5 to "joined by the grouping id". (3) **Store the trigger code (`owner` \| `scheduled` \| `external`), not the two-value owner / automatic** — still one short code; "automatic" is derived for display. Slice 12's stranger cap (SA-S7) counts charged **`external`** actions per account per day, and "automatic" merges `external` with the nightly runs; counting by action type instead would silently miss the next stranger-triggered type. Also for the slice 3 workplan: decide the **adjustment-row shape now** (a row kind plus a nullable reference to the adjusted action, or a sibling table) so slice 4 adds rows, not a breaking migration; adjustment rows have their own id and never reuse the adjusted row's `action_id`. The diary reading only the charge table is confirmed (SA-S5). *(BA, 2026-09-28: folded into §4, Scenario 1 step 5, FR-10, FR-13, AC-11, NFR Correctness / Auditability and slices 3, 4 and 12.)*
- [x] **SQ-16 — A failed call that spent tokens is recorded at $0** (raised by: BA | status: ruled 2026-09-28 | blocks: slice 2 workplan). SA N-3 found that `baseProvider.ts:166-176` records a failed provider call at $0 even when the provider billed for it; the user wants this assessed and fixed in slice 2. `baseProvider` is shared with the agent platform. *BA suggested resolution:* where the provider's error response reports usage, record that usage and its cost; where it does not, keep $0 and record it as a known issue (KI-8). SA to confirm that the agent platform's charge is token-based (A-2) and does not read the recorded cost of failed calls, so the fix cannot change what an agent run costs — otherwise the fix is limited to Business OS calls. **SA ruling (TECHNICAL):** **no code change; KI-8 is accepted with SA sign-off, and `baseProvider.ts` is not touched in slice 2.** Verified: (a) **there is nothing to record** — on failure the OpenAI SDK throws an `APIError` carrying `status`, `headers`, `error` (the error body), `code`, `param`, `type` and `requestID`, and **no usage** (`openai/core/error.d.ts:3-17`); `APIConnectionError` / `APIUserAbortError` (timeouts, aborts) carry even less. The provider's accounting of a failed or timed-out generation never reaches us, so "where the error reports usage" is an empty set for the provider Business OS uses, and a fix would be dead code in a shared file. (b) **A-2 confirmed:** the agent platform's charge is token-based and in-memory — `run-agent` passes `executionResult.totalTokensUsed` (`app/api/run-agent/route.ts:372`, `:573`) to `CreditService.chargeTokensWithIntensity`; it does not read `token_usage.cost_usd`. **But** agent-side readers do sum failed-row **tokens** from `token_usage` (`TokenReconciliationService.ts:78`, `updateAgentIntensity.ts:365`), so changing what the shared failure path records would shift agent reconciliation and intensity figures even though no agent charge moves — a second reason to leave it alone. AC-29's "documented as a known issue with SA sign-off" branch applies; this ruling is the sign-off. BA to restate KI-8's status as "accepted — the provider does not report usage on an error (SQ-16)", and add to slice 2's guardrails: no change to the `baseProvider.ts` failure path. Revisit only if a provider Business OS is configured to use starts returning usage on errors. *(BA, 2026-09-28: folded into FR-12h, AC-29, slice 2 and KI-8.)*

**New questions from the 2026-09-29 user decisions** (each with a BA-suggested resolution):

- [x] **SQ-17 — The allowance id at slice 5: `ai.actions` or a general credit allowance?** (raised by: user via BA, 2026-09-29 | status: open — **to be ruled by SA at the start of slice 5**, not now | blocks: slice 5 only). SA-S11 said to keep the capability **id** `ai.actions` because admin overrides stored in the database reference it, and to change only its `unit` and labels. After BD-12 the allowance is the one pool for every chargeable service, and the user wants it to become a **general credit allowance** when allowances are set in slice 5. *BA suggested resolution:* in slice 5, change the capability's `unit`, labels and description to a neutral credit allowance in any case (BD-15). Rename the **id** as well (for example to a neutral `credits` id) **only if** the override rows that reference `ai.actions` can be migrated in the same change, with a check that none is left behind and no admin screen or cohort still reads the old id; otherwise keep the id, neutralise everything the owner and admins see, and record the id as a known naming debt. SA decides which, and whether a transitional alias is needed. *(Note 2026-09-30: the derived numbers (D1–D3) do not depend on this ruling — 19,750 / 32,250 credits apply under either id. One BA observation for SA: any existing `add_override` rows on `ai.actions` were set in **action counts** against the old placeholders, so whichever id SA chooses, those override values need a look when the unit becomes credits — an override of, say, 1,000 would now mean 1,000 credits, well below the new plan allowances.)* **Ruled by SA in the slice 5 review (2026-09-30), status now ruled:** rename `ai.actions` → **`credits.allowance`**, unit **`'credit'`**, neutral labels, **no transitional alias**, no `removals` entry — this supersedes SA-S11's "keep the id". SA measured **0** override rows on `ai.actions`, so the BA's override observation has nothing to act on today; SA's condition C-3 re-counts before merge. Full text in [SA review — slice 5](#sa-review--slice-5-2026-09-30). *(BA, 2026-09-30: folded into FR-37, AC-25, slice 5, §13's allowance-naming row, OI-7 and KI-13. Previous status, superseded: "open — to be ruled by SA at the start of slice 5, not now".)*
- [x] **SQ-18 — The format of the `service` identifier** (raised by: BA, 2026-09-29 | status: **ruled by SA 2026-09-29** (SA re-check of the neutral ledger) | blocked: the slice 3b migration — now unblocked). BD-13 says `service` is "an identifier format, not a fixed list". *BA suggested resolution:* a required text column with a database check on its shape — lowercase letters, digits and underscores, starting with a letter, short (for example up to 40 characters) — not an enum and not a lookup table, so a new service needs no migration; the AI path writes the literal `'ai'` from a single constant, never from a request. The totals table has no `service` column (one pool). SA to confirm the shape and the length limit, and whether the record function itself validates the value in addition to the check. **SA ruling (TECHNICAL, 2026-09-29):** `service` is **required on charge rows, NULL on adjustments (inherited)**; format **lowercase ASCII letter first, then letters/digits/underscore, 1–64 chars (the same rule as action_type)**; **not a closed list (no enum or registry — a new service needs no migration)**; **the table CHECK is the single format source**; **service ids are permanent and never reused for a different meaning**. Condition **C-6:** **3b must write `'ai'` from one exported constant.** *(BA, 2026-09-29: this departs from the BA suggestion in two places — the length limit is 64, not 40, and adjustment rows carry NULL rather than a required value — and answers the "does the record function also validate" question: the CHECK is the single source of the format, so no second definition is written. Folded into §4, FR-13, FR-40b, NFR Correctness / Testability, AC-11, AC-34, slice 3, BD-13, rule 3 of [Adding a future chargeable service](#adding-a-future-chargeable-service) and OI-8.)* *(Previous status, superseded 2026-09-29: "status: open — for the slice 3b workplan or its SA code review | blocks: the slice 3b migration".)*

**New questions from the 2026-09-30 user decisions** (each with a BA-suggested resolution):

- [x] **SQ-19 — Does slice 5 bump the tier-matrix `version`, and which `matrixVersion` does credit value version 1 carry?** (raised by: BA, 2026-09-30 | status: open — **for SA at the start of slice 5** | blocks: slice 5 only). SQ-7 ruled that the credit value is "versioned with the matrix", and each `CreditValueVersion` records the `matrixVersion` it was decided against. Version 0 carries `matrixVersion: 1`. Slice 5 **raises** both allowances (500 → 19,750; 2,000 → 32,250), and under the entitlements rules a raise is one line and needs **no** version bump — only a lowering does. So as written, credit value version 1 would also carry `matrixVersion: 1`, and "matrix version 1" would name both the placeholder allowances and the derived ones. *BA suggested resolution:* bump `TIER_MATRIX.version` to **2** in slice 5, with no `removals` entry (nothing is lowered), and have credit value version 1 carry `matrixVersion: 2`, so the pair (credit value v1, matrix v2) identifies the derived set unambiguously and a later reader of a charge row can tell which allowances were in force. SA to confirm whether a bump with no removal is permitted by the snapshot test and has no side effect on `plan_version` / grandfathering for existing plan rows; if it does, keep version 1 and record the pairing in the credit value's derivation string instead. **Ruled by SA in the slice 5 review (2026-09-30), status now ruled:** as the BA suggested — `TIER_MATRIX.version` **1 → 2**, `removals` stays empty, credit value version 1 carries **`matrixVersion: 2`**; SA checked the snapshot test, `creditValue.test.ts`, plan rows and grandfathering, cohorts, shadow events and the admin display, and found no side effect. Full text in [SA review — slice 5](#sa-review--slice-5-2026-09-30). *(BA, 2026-09-30: folded into FR-37, AC-25 and slice 5. Previous status, superseded: "open — for SA at the start of slice 5".)*

**New questions from the slice 6 scoping (2026-09-30)** (each with a BA-suggested resolution; all block only slice 6):

- [ ] **SQ-20 — Which period the card reads, and the trial's total** (raised by: BA, 2026-09-30 | status: open — for the slice 6 workplan | blocks: slice 6a). The card must read exactly the totals row its charges were written into. *BA suggested resolution:* compute the current `period_start` with the **same function** the charge recorder uses to stamp `period_start` (slice 3), from the account's `period_anchor` (T-6); never a second computation in the route. The reset date shown is the next period's start. For the trial, "used" is the sum of every totals row since the trial began (a 14-day trial can straddle two periods), and no reset date is shown. For an account with no plan row, use whatever the recorder uses for such an account, so the card and the ledger agree.
- [ ] **SQ-21 — The owner's read path** (raised by: BA, 2026-09-30 | status: open | blocks: slice 6a). Either the owner's RLS client (owner-SELECT exists on both ledger tables; per-column grants already hide the cost columns from owners — slice 3) with `.eq('user_id', userId)`, or `BusinessOsCreditLedgerReadRepository` on the service role (built for admin readers; its header notes a later owner reader can pass the RLS client). *BA suggested resolution:* a repository method called with the owner's RLS client, selecting only credits, kind, trigger, the adjusted action id and `period_start` — so the database itself guarantees an owner never reads a cost column (FR-28), and no service-role read is added to an owner route. SA to confirm which columns an owner may SELECT.
- [ ] **SQ-22 — Where the "by you / automatic" split comes from** (raised by: BA, 2026-09-30 | status: open | blocks: slice 6a). The totals row has one figure per account per period and no trigger. *BA suggested resolution:* sum the period's rows by **effective trigger** in Node, through `lib/business-os/credits/effectiveFields.ts` — an adjustment takes the trigger of the charge it corrects, as N-10 does for `service` — with no migration; rows per period are in the tens today. Move the sum into an RPC only if an account reaches thousands of rows a period. If the split and the totals row disagree, the totals row wins for "used" and the mismatch is logged at `warn`.
- [ ] **SQ-23 — `usageSummary.ts` and the admin LLM usage report's Check 5** (raised by: BA, 2026-09-30 | status: open | blocks: slice 6a). `usageSummary.ts` exists so that Check 5 computes "exactly what the owner's usage card shows" (its header; Layer 1.1 FR-23). After slice 6 the card no longer shows that. *BA suggested resolution:* keep `usageSummary.ts` for the admin report, reword Check 5's claim to "token usage by feature", and add no new link between the admin report and the card.
- [ ] **SQ-24 — How refresh trigger (a) reaches the card** (raised by: BA, 2026-09-30 | status: open | blocks: slice 6b). The owner's AI actions (chat, briefing refresh, website regeneration) run in other components. *BA suggested resolution:* one small, documented client-side signal that the owner's AI surfaces raise when an action finishes and the card listens for — only on the page the card is on; a page without the card needs nothing, because the card reads fresh when the owner comes back (trigger b). A new client pattern, so SA rules (CLAUDE.md rule 7).
- [ ] **SQ-25 — The credits line's category (N-12)** (raised by: BA, 2026-09-30 | status: open — only if the user picks D-g B or C | blocks: nothing else). The technical side of D-g: a new catalog category, its heading key in three languages, the category-order test, under the `business-os-entitlements` skill's rules. *BA suggested resolution:* a category `credits`, placed first in `CATEGORY_PRESENTATION`.
- [ ] **SQ-26 — Internal and platform accounts** (raised by: BA, 2026-09-30 | status: open). *BA suggested resolution:* no special case in the owner surface; an operator or test account shows whatever its own plan row resolves to, or the no-plan-row state. SA to confirm nothing in the card or route should know an account is internal.
- [ ] **SQ-27 — Room for grants in the payload** (raised by: BA, 2026-09-30 | status: open). *BA suggested resolution:* "remaining" is computed on the server as allowance + live grants (0 until slice 11) − used, and the payload's shape already has a place for granted credits, so slice 11 adds a value rather than a new shape. SA picks the field.
- [ ] **SQ-28 — The route itself** (raised by: BA, 2026-09-30 | status: open | blocks: slice 6a). Replace `/api/business-os/usage` in place, or add a sibling and retire the old one; drop `range`, `breakdown`, `daily` and `calls`. *BA suggested resolution:* SA greps for other consumers; if the card is the only one, replace in place. The response is never cached — consumption is read fresh (SQ-5).

### SA correction C-1 (slice 1 workplan review, 2026-09-28)

**Raised by:** SA, reviewing the slice 1 Dev workplan. **A correction of fact, not a reopened decision.** Recorded here by BA and folded into the requirement.

- **The fact:** the lead reply (`lead_reply_recommendation`) **already has a template fallback.** Every failure in `recommendLeadReply` falls back to `pickFallbackCandidate` — a fixed rule, with no AI call (`LeadReplyRecommender.ts`, `leadReplyCandidates.ts`). The model only ever **picks which of the owner's links to send**; it never writes the reply. `LeadAlertService` returns **before** the AI action when there are no candidates, so such a lead makes no AI call and is never charged.
- **What was wrong:** OI-1, slice 1's scope ("recorded as **missing**"), slice 10's scope ("build the missing template fallback") and FR-20 ("this layer builds one") all said the fallback was missing. So did SA's own D-0 scoping, item 2 ("when the model returns nothing, no reply is queued") — **SA's D-0 item 2 is corrected by C-1.**
- **What changes:** slice 1 records the fallback as **`exists`**; FR-20 and slice 10 now **route "balance exhausted" down the existing fallback path the area-switched-off path already uses**, rather than building one; OI-1 is closed. The BA's original wording is kept, marked superseded, where it appeared.
- **What does not change:** no decision is reopened. BD-5 (degrade, don't stop), FR-19 and AC-14 stand; the lead reply still goes out with template text at zero.

### SA re-check of the neutral ledger (2026-09-29)

**Raised by:** SA, re-checking the neutral ledger of BD-12 to BD-15. **Rulings and corrections, not a reopened decision.** Recorded here by BA with SA's text as given, and folded into the requirement.

| # | SA text | Folded into |
|---|---|---|
| **N-10** | Adjustment rows carry no `service` of their own; they inherit it from the charge they adjust. A plain `WHERE service = 'ai'` skips adjustments. FR-33's note, slice 4's scope (the leak check, the operator report) and slice 7's diary use the **effective service** = the row's service, else the service of the charge it adjusts. | §4 (the effective service), §5, FR-13, FR-26, FR-33, FR-40b / f, NFR Correctness / Testability, AC-11, AC-34, slices 3, 4 and 7 |
| **N-11** | An action type only has meaning within its service. Lookups of area, audience and diary label key on (service, action type) — or action types must be unique across services; a new service must not reuse an existing action type name. | Rule 9 of [Adding a future chargeable service](#adding-a-future-chargeable-service), FR-4 note, FR-26, FR-40f, slice 7 |
| **SQ-18** (ruling) | `service` is required on charge rows, NULL on adjustments (inherited); format lowercase ASCII letter first, then letters/digits/underscore, 1–64 chars (the same rule as action_type); not a closed list (no enum or registry — a new service needs no migration); the table CHECK is the single format source; service ids are permanent and never reused for a different meaning. | SQ-18 (marked ruled), §4, FR-13, FR-40b, NFR Correctness, AC-34, slice 3, BD-13, rule 3, OI-8 |
| **C-6** (condition) | 3b must write `'ai'` from one exported constant. | Slice 3 (scope and guardrails), FR-13, NFR Security, AC-34, rule 3, OI-8 |
| **S-3** (text alignment) | The `is_fallback_priced` flag means "priced from a fallback rate rather than the measured one; AI sets it when a model is missing from the price table; another service sets it only if it defines its own documented fallback". | §4 (the thin row), FR-12b, "What differs from AI" under [Adding a future chargeable service](#adding-a-future-chargeable-service). The requirement already said this in substance; the wording is now aligned |

**What does not change:** BD-12 to BD-15, the one pool, the neutral names, the required grouping id and the one totals row per account per period all stand. No slice is added. SQ-17 stays open for slice 5. *(2026-09-30: SQ-17 has since been ruled in the slice 5 SA review.)*

**New questions from the slice 11 scoping (2026-10-02)** (each with a BA-suggested resolution; they block slice 11 only unless stated):

- [x] **S11-SQ-1 — The lots table, and the T-2 / R-5 acknowledgement** (raised by: BA, 2026-10-02 | status: open | blocks: 11a, and boost slice 2). T-2 lists `id, user_id, source, credits_granted numeric(18,6) > 0, credits_base, credits_bonus, credit_value_version, expires_at NULL, idempotency_key UNIQUE NOT NULL, source_ref, actor, reason, created_at`, and assumed boost would create the table with `'boost_purchase'` and slice 11 would add `'admin_grant'` "through a CHECK change". Slice 11 now creates it. *BA suggested resolution:* (a) SA confirms, for the credit deduction requirement, that slice 11 adopts T-2 and R-5 (a)–(c) in full (Fragment A §2) — this is the acknowledgement boost RV-6 / R-5 is waiting for; (b) the `source` CHECK accepts **both** `'admin_grant'` and `'boost_purchase'` from day one, so boost needs no CHECK change; (c) a per-source shape CHECK, like the charges table's `kind` shape CHECKs (`20261015…sql:34-36`): for `'admin_grant'`, `reason` required (≥ 3 characters), the actor is an admin user id, `source_ref` NULL, `credits_base = credits_granted` and `credits_bonus = 0`; for `'boost_purchase'`, whatever boost needs, decided now so boost adds no column; (d) the actor stored as a typed admin id column plus an actor kind (`admin` / `stripe_webhook`) rather than one free-text column; (e) the admin idempotency key is `'admin_grant:' ∥ request id`, where the request id is a UUID the admin UI mints once per form submission and sends in the body (validated as a UUID; it identifies the attempt, never the account or the amount), so a double-click or a retried request records one lot. **SA ruling (2026-10-02):** Adopted in full; both sources from day one; per-source shape CHECKs; typed actor (`actor_kind` + `actor_admin_id`, no FK); key `'admin_grant:' ∥ requestId`; a reused key on a different account / amount raises → 409 `idempotency_key_conflict`. See [SA review — slice 11 scoping](#sa-review--slice-11-scoping-2026-10-02).
- [x] **S11-SQ-2 — The reversal table's name and shape** (raised by: BA | status: open | blocks: 11a). T-2 suggests "for example a `business_os_credit_lot_draws` table written by slices 9–10 and 11". *BA suggested resolution:* **one** generic append-only table for every movement out of a lot — `business_os_credit_lot_draws` with a `kind` (`'reversal'` now; `'consumption'` added for slices 9–10), `lot_id`, `user_id`, positive `credits`, `reason` (required for reversals), actor, idempotency key, `created_at` — so a lot's remaining is one sum, and boost's manual clawback (T-9) reuses it. Whether the `kind` CHECK includes `'consumption'` now (no writer yet) or slice 9 adds it is SA's call; BA leans to including it now, to save a migration on a money table, given T-2's caution against columns "before a writer exists" applies to mutable columns, not to an allowed value. **SA ruling (2026-10-02):** One table `business_os_credit_lot_draws`, but `kind` admits `'reversal'` only; slice 9 / 10 adds `'consumption'` with its columns (at least `period_start`) — BA lean overruled. See [SA review — slice 11 scoping](#sa-review--slice-11-scoping-2026-10-02).
- [x] **S11-SQ-3 — Consumption draws: now, or with slice 9** (raised by: BA | status: open | blocks: slice 9, not 11). *BA suggested resolution:* slice 11 writes **no** consumption rows. Slice 9 / 10 decides who writes them and when — at charge time through a separate function (lots must never touch `business_os_record_credit_charge`, T-2), or once per period from the totals row. **No backfill:** overspend in `shadow` before draws exist is not drawn from lots retroactively (S11-KI-2). **SA ruling (2026-10-02):** Confirmed: no consumption rows and no backfill in slice 11; slice 9 / 10 writes them outside the charge function, under the same per-account advisory lock; SA recommends R-5's explicit allowance-then-lots branch. See [SA review — slice 11 scoping](#sa-review--slice-11-scoping-2026-10-02).
- [x] **S11-SQ-4 — What `granted` means while nothing draws, and for a mid-period grant** (raised by: BA | status: open | blocks: 11c, 11d, slice 9). R-5 (b) says `granted` is "the lot balance at the start of the period (lots granted minus draws recorded in earlier periods)". Read literally, a lot created on the 20th would not count until the next period. *BA suggested resolution:* `granted` = the sum over the account's **unexpired** lots of credits granted − reversals − draws recorded in **earlier** periods, **including lots created during the current period**; while no draws exist that is simply credits granted − reversals on unexpired lots. A lot that expires mid-period stops counting from its expiry instant. SA rules the exact rule for slice 9's enforcement; 11c and 11d use the same function, so the admin, the owner and enforcement read one number. **SA ruling (2026-10-02):** BA reading confirmed as the definition: extra credits = Σ (granted − reversals − consumption) over lots unexpired at `at`, mid-period lots counted from creation; R-5 (b)'s "start of the period" qualifies the draws subtracted. One pure function `creditLots.ts` (11a). See [SA review — slice 11 scoping](#sa-review--slice-11-scoping-2026-10-02).
- [x] **S11-SQ-5 — Same op union or a sibling** (raised by: BA | status: open | blocks: 11b). *BA suggested resolution:* the same `POST /api/admin/business-os/entitlements/accounts/[accountId]` and the same `adminOpSchema` union, so the gate, Zod, tenant check, plan-row check, audit and WC-7 flush are reused (FR-30 says "through the existing admin operations path"). The new ops' executor lives in a sibling file in `lib/business-os/credits/` (the lots belong to the credit ledger, not to entitlements), dispatched from `executeAdminOp`. Two things to rule: (a) the route's audit call hard-codes `entityType: 'business_os_account_plan'` and plan-row before / after (`route.ts:242-246`); the new ops need `entityType` `'business_os_credit_lot'` and before / after lot balances, so the outcome type gains those fields; (b) the entitlements registration rule for the new import (S11-SQ-15). Ops: `grant_credits` and `reduce_credit_lot` (with "the rest" ending it), rather than a third `end_*` op. **SA ruling (2026-10-02):** Same route and union; the two variants and executor live in `lib/business-os/credits/creditAdminOps.ts` (no import from entitlements); `AdminOpOutcome` gains 403, an audit override, `replayed` (no audit row) and no cache invalidation for credit ops. See [SA review — slice 11 scoping](#sa-review--slice-11-scoping-2026-10-02).
- [x] **S11-SQ-6 — The self-account guard: where, and for which ops** (raised by: BA | status: open | blocks: 11b). Both ids are already in the executor context (`adminOps.ts:164-167`) and never compared. *BA suggested resolution:* the check sits in `executeAdminOp`, before any read, comparing `resolveAccountId(adminId)` with the target, with an explicit code (for example `own_account`, 403). BA suggests it applies to **all** entitlement ops, not only the credit ones: `add_override` on one's own `credits.allowance` is a self-grant by another name, and FR-30's intent is that no admin changes their own entitlement. That changes the 7 existing ops (today an admin can change their own plan), so SA decides; if SA limits it to the credit ops, record the remaining hole as a known issue. **SA ruling (2026-10-02):** Guard first in `executeAdminOp`, 403 `own_account`, for the two credit ops in 11b; extending it to the 7 existing ops goes to the user as S11-BQ-1. See [SA review — slice 11 scoping](#sa-review--slice-11-scoping-2026-10-02).
- [x] **S11-SQ-7 — Tenant isolation on the service-role write** (raised by: BA | status: open | blocks: 11b). The account comes from the URL path, the write runs on the service role, and the RLS does not protect it. *BA suggested resolution:* follow the `tenant-isolation-guard` skill: `isBusinessOsTenant` and the plan-row check as today (`adminOps.ts:217-234`); a reduction looks up the lot **scoped to the account**, as `endOverride` does with overrides (`adminOps.ts:490-494`), so a lot id from another account gets 404; the record functions take the user id as an argument and the reversal function checks the lot belongs to it inside the transaction. **SA ruling (2026-10-02):** As suggested, plus the platform-account refusal, account only from the path, lot read within the account, the function re-checks ownership under its lock, and a foreign-lot test that never reaches the function. See [SA review — slice 11 scoping](#sa-review--slice-11-scoping-2026-10-02).
- [x] **S11-SQ-8 — Which migration number** (raised by: BA | status: open | blocks: 11a). Free numbers for credit deduction: 20261017, 20261018, 20261019 (20261016 reserved for slice 4c). *BA suggested resolution:* **20261017** for the lots table, the draws / reversal table and their functions, in one migration; 20261018 kept for a slice 11 follow-up if SA needs one; 20261019 left for slices 9–10's draw writer if it needs schema. Recorded in both requirements (Fragment G): boost slice 2's migration (20261030) creates only its purchase and cap-override tables and functions. **SA ruling (2026-10-02):** 20261017 (both tables and both functions); 20261018 held for a slice 11 follow-up; 20261019 held for slice 9 / 10 consumption draws. See [SA review — slice 11 scoping](#sa-review--slice-11-scoping-2026-10-02).
- [x] **S11-SQ-9 — How the admin view gets its figures** (raised by: BA | status: open | blocks: 11c). *BA suggested resolution:* a **new** admin read route under `/api/admin/business-os/credits/accounts/[accountId]` (`requireAdmin` first, Zod on the id), so the entitlements GET payload that two screens consume stays as it is. Allowance and deciding layer from the entitlements snapshot (`values['credits.allowance']`, `decidedBy`); period from `creditPeriod.ts` (the same function the card and the recorder use); used from the totals row through the admin ledger read repository; lots and reversals through `BusinessOsCreditLotRepository`; the balance from `computeCreditBalance` with `granted` per S11-SQ-4. The period and sums logic of `ownerCreditUsage.ts` is shared, not copied (it is written against the owner RLS client today; SA says how to reuse it with an admin client). **SA ruling (2026-10-02):** New `GET /api/admin/business-os/credits/accounts/[accountId]`; `readOwnerCreditUsage`'s body extracted to `readCreditPosition` and reused with an explicit, documented service-role owner read repository; no combined remaining (S11-CR-3). See [SA review — slice 11 scoping](#sa-review--slice-11-scoping-2026-10-02).
- [x] **S11-SQ-10 — The owner payload and the owner read** (raised by: BA | status: open | blocks: 11d). *BA suggested resolution:* one numeric key for all lots (per S11-D-1), filled from the owner RLS client under the owner column-level grant; the allow-list pin updated deliberately (boost RV-5). Note `computeCreditBalance` returns `null` when there is no allowance, even if `granted` > 0 (`creditBalance.ts:31`); since grants to accounts with no plan row are refused (S11-D-3), the case should not arise, but SA says whether the function should change. **SA ruling (2026-10-02):** `granted` replaced by `extraCredits`; `remaining` stays plan-only; owner reads via new owner-repository methods; `computeCreditBalance` unchanged in slice 11. See [SA review — slice 11 scoping](#sa-review--slice-11-scoping-2026-10-02).
- [x] **S11-SQ-11 — Audit events** (raised by: BA | status: open | blocks: 11b). *BA suggested resolution:* new `AUDIT_EVENTS` keys (for example `BOS_CREDIT_GRANTED`, `BOS_CREDIT_LOT_REDUCED`), registered so `adminOps.test.ts`'s sweep of action literals passes; severity `warning` like the other entitlement ops; details carry the lot id, credits, expiry, reason, idempotency key and correlation id — never owner text. **SA ruling (2026-10-02):** `BOS_CREDIT_LOT_GRANTED` / `BOS_CREDIT_LOT_REDUCED`, severity `warning`, entity `business_os_credit_lot`, before / after from the reversal function; a replay writes no audit row. See [SA review — slice 11 scoping](#sa-review--slice-11-scoping-2026-10-02).
- [x] **S11-SQ-12 — Registries and retention** (raised by: BA | status: open | blocks: 11a). *BA suggested resolution:* as boost F-11 / NFR-12 and the charges precedent: `user_id` FK to `auth.users` with `ON DELETE SET NULL`; registered in `businessOwnedTables.ts`, `purge/descriptors.ts` (never purged — financial record) and `accountDeletionPolicy.ts` (minimise); a business Reset does not touch lots. **SA ruling (2026-10-02):** As suggested, for both tables (owned-tables, purge `never`, deletion `minimise`). See [SA review — slice 11 scoping](#sa-review--slice-11-scoping-2026-10-02).
- [x] **S11-SQ-13 — Where the typo ceiling lives** (raised by: BA | status: open — only if the user picks S11-D-5 B | blocks: 11b). *BA suggested resolution:* one exported constant in `lib/business-os/credits/`, read by the Zod schema (`.max(...)`), with a test; whether it is also a CHECK on the table is SA's call (BA: no, the ceiling is a policy, not a fact about the data). **SA ruling (2026-10-02):** One exported constant read by Zod, whole credits, no table CHECK; a 10,000,000 sanity ceiling remains if the user picks S11-D-5 A. See [SA review — slice 11 scoping](#sa-review--slice-11-scoping-2026-10-02).
- [x] **S11-SQ-14 — Expiry as a date** (raised by: BA | status: open | blocks: 11b, 11c). An admin picks a date; the column is an instant. *BA suggested resolution:* the admin form sends an ISO instant; the UI turns a picked date into the **end of that day in UTC** and says so beside the field ("ends 23:59 UTC on …"); an expiry in the past is refused (400). **SA ruling (2026-10-02):** As suggested; server refuses an expiry at or before now (400); table CHECK `expires_at > created_at` as backstop; admin view shows UTC, labelled. See [SA review — slice 11 scoping](#sa-review--slice-11-scoping-2026-10-02).
- [x] **S11-SQ-15 — Entitlements registration** (raised by: BA | status: open | blocks: 11b, 11c). Any new file that imports from `lib/business-os/entitlements/`, type-only included, is registered per the `business-os-entitlements` skill in the same change; the credit executor and the admin view route are non-gate importers. **SA ruling (2026-10-02):** 11b creates no new importer if built as ruled; 11c registers the view route; the allowance-and-layer helper lives inside the module; `test:bos-entitlements` green per sub-slice. See [SA review — slice 11 scoping](#sa-review--slice-11-scoping-2026-10-02).
- [x] **S11-SQ-16 — Admin access register and CI guard** (raised by: BA | status: open | blocks: 11b, 11c). Any new admin route has `requireAdmin` as its first statement (the required `Admin authz surface guard`), is added to the admin access register, and write routes are registered in `adminGate.writes` if the extended POST needs it. **SA ruling (2026-10-02):** New GET gated first, registered, census and guard caps moved by one handler in the same PR; 11b adds no handler. See [SA review — slice 11 scoping](#sa-review--slice-11-scoping-2026-10-02).

## 15. Operator questions answered as-built

The user's two queued questions, answered against the code first.

**"Can an admin add or reduce a customer's credit?"** — **No, nowhere, on either side.**
- Business OS: the admin op union has no credit operation (A-14), and nothing counts AI usage, so there is nothing to grant. An admin *can* raise an account's `ai.actions` allowance with `add_override`, but with `ALWAYS_SUFFICIENT` behind `balance.ts` (A-10) the value has no effect. *(Note 2026-09-30: after slice 5 the capability is `credits.allowance` — SQ-17.)*
- Agent platform: no admin route writes a balance (A-14). A Pilot-Credit balance moves only by the signup grant, an agent-run charge, or a Stripe purchase (`CreditService.ts:30-82`, `:444`).
- **The gap is FR-30 (grant / reduce, audited) and FR-31 (read the position)** — slice 11. It is needed before enforcement, not after: the champion policy is literally "when they hit the cap, an admin grants credits" (D-7), and that path does not exist.

**"Is there a subscription-expiry lock?"** — **Half of one, and it fails open (A-15).**
- Agent platform: `checkExecutionAllowed` blocks an agent run on `account_frozen` or insufficient balance, but returns `allowed: true` on any DB error or a missing subscription row; and the cron that would set `account_frozen` is not scheduled, so in practice nothing freezes.
- Business OS: the lifecycle (trial → grace → paused) and per-account expiry exist and resolve correctly, but the mode is `shadow`, so an expired account is refused nothing (A-13).
- **The gap is FR-32** (slice 12): an expired Business OS plan must stop owner-facing AI through the entitlements lifecycle — explicitly **not** by reusing `account_frozen`, which is an agent-platform flag also read by agent generation and would freeze a paying Business OS customer.

## 16. Out of scope

| Item | Where |
|---|---|
| Boost purchase, auto top-up, add-ons, annual pricing | S-4b |
| Enforcement switch-on and G-1 key rotation | S-5 |
| Reconciling our cost figure against the provider's invoice; cached-input and batch rates | Follow-up. FR-12 removes silent $0 and flags a guessed price; it does not claim our figure equals OpenAI's bill |
| Any change to Pilot Credits, `CreditService`, `pricingConfig` or the agent charge path | Forbidden here (A-11); retirement is WS-3 |
| SMS metering and email fair-use | Same mechanism, later — a credit extends to them. *(Refined 2026-09-29 by BD-12: see the three rows below.)* |
| **Designing or pricing any non-AI chargeable service** (notification email, SMS, …) — how its cost is measured, what its provider charges, its action types, audiences and diary labels | Later, one service at a time (BD-12). Must follow [Adding a future chargeable service](#adding-a-future-chargeable-service) |
| **Building the wrapper a non-AI service needs** (its equivalent of `runAiAction`: action id per chargeable action, required grouping id, charge through `business_os_record_credit_charge`) | Built with the first non-AI service (BD-12). This layer builds only the neutral ledger that wrapper will write to |
| **A reconciliation / leak check for a non-AI service** | With that service. Slice 4's leak check covers AI only, because it compares against `token_usage` |
| **A "new chargeable service" skill** | Written together with the first non-AI service, when its wrapper exists (OI-6) |
| An all-businesses usage overview | Layer 1.1 F-2 |
| Making AI audit entries durable (Layer 3 KI-B / OI-D) | Separate; FR-15 removes the billing dependency on it |
| A backfill of charges missed by the leak check | Decided after slice 4 measures the loss rate (SQ-3, N-7) |
| Any second tracking mechanism | Out by the user's reuse decision (§3, BD-10) |
| Background or timer polling of the usage card | Out by BD-11 (FR-39) |
| Recording usage for a failed provider call | Out by SQ-16 — the provider reports none on an error (KI-8) |
| A separate allowance, balance or totals row per service | Out by BD-12 / BD-13 — one pool, one totals row per account per period |
| A service registry, enum or lookup table for `service` values | Out by the SQ-18 ruling — the table CHECK is the single format source, and a new service needs no migration |
| *(added 2026-09-30)* A markup applied to the charge itself, or a retail "price per credit" shown to owners | Out by D1 — the charge stays measured cost ÷ the credit value; the markup is expressed only in allowance size. Owners never see dollars (FR-28) |
| *(added 2026-09-30)* Changing plan fees ($79 / $129) or which plans exist | Pricing, not this layer. A fee change is a revisit trigger for the allowances (pricing doc §6.1) |
| *(added 2026-09-30)* The `console.*` conversion of `lib/business-os/LanguageContext.tsx` | Done by a separate change, merged as PR #155 — not part of slice 5 or of this layer |

## 17. Known issues and open items

| Id | Description | Status |
|---|---|---|
| **KI-1** | **The same action costs different amounts on different days**, and support cannot quote a price in advance. Accepted with BD-1; mitigated by the diary (FR-26) and the own-history range (FR-27) | **Accepted by the user, 2026-09-28** |
| **KI-2** | A fallback-priced charge (FR-12b) is a **deliberate over-estimate**, so an owner can be charged slightly more than we actually paid until it is reconciled. Flagged, counted and reconciled — never silent | Accepted; watch the count |
| **KI-3** | The image reuse cache means the ledger charges **generations**, not requests; a repeated request is free and absent from the diary. Consistent with FR-7, but anyone auditing image spend must know it | Informational (Layer 1.5 KI-B) |
| **KI-4** | Two dormant action types (`website_section_field_rewrite`, `website_block_enrichment`) have no production trigger. They must be declared and given a diary label anyway, or they arrive unlabelled the day they are wired | Declared in slice 1 (was D-0) |
| **KI-5** | A small bounded overspend is possible under a concurrent burst (SQ-4), and under option B a pre-check cannot know what the next action will cost | Accepted (SA ruling SQ-4): shown as 0, never carried into the next period (§5) |
| **KI-6** | Chat v2 and v1 are outside the call catalog and choose their own model; their spend is not in the eight areas and so is not chargeable by FR-4 | Pre-existing (Layer 2); state it, do not fix it here. The slice 4 leak check will show it as uncharged spend if any Business OS account uses them |
| **KI-7** | An image is priced as `high` when the provider reports no quality (Layer 1.5 KI-E), so its recorded cost is an upper bound. Under option B that upper bound is **charged**; frequent warnings mean owners are being charged conservatively | Informational; watch the warning count |
| **KI-8** | A failed provider call is recorded at $0 (`baseProvider.ts:166-176`), so a failure the provider billed anyway is not charged (SA N-3). **The provider reports no usage on an error** — the OpenAI SDK's `APIError` carries no usage, and timeouts / aborts carry less — so there is nothing to record. `baseProvider.ts`'s failure path is not changed | **Accepted with SA sign-off (SQ-16, 2026-09-28)** — revisit only if a provider Business OS uses starts returning usage on errors. *(Superseded status: "Assessed and, where the provider reports usage, fixed in slice 2 (SQ-16); any residue stays accepted".)* |
| **KI-9** | **Cached input is priced at OpenAI's half rate for OpenAI calls** — `openaiProvider.ts` (`calculateCost`, `:552-571`) already does this — and at the full input rate for other providers, where those are charged slightly more (conservative). *(Superseded description, stale per SA follow-up: "Provider-cached input tokens are charged at the full input rate (`cachedInputTokens` is recorded but not priced), so the owner is slightly over-charged — conservative (SA N-4)".)* | Accepted; restated 2026-09-28. Cached-input pricing for other providers is out of scope (§16) |
| **KI-10** | The AI audit trail is lossy by design and is archived by the Admin Archiving module, so it can never be the bill (§3). Anyone comparing audit entries with charges will occasionally find an audit entry missing | Informational; the charge table is authoritative for money. *(2026-09-30: observed once in the benchmark — an insight-run charge on 2026-09-30 03:31 UTC with no matching audit entry, while a run seconds earlier on another account had one; recorded in the pricing doc's caveats.)* |
| **KI-11** | If slice 6 (card) reaches owners before slice 7 (diary), owners see a falling number with no per-action explanation | BA recommends releasing 6 and 7 together (§12) |
| **KI-12** | *(added 2026-09-28)* With no background polling (BD-11), work that runs automatically while the owner's page is open — the nightly insight run, the briefing, a lead reply — does not appear on the card until the next refresh (the owner's own action, a return to the tab, or the refresh icon) | **Accepted by the user, 2026-09-28** (BD-11) |
| **KI-13** | *(added 2026-09-29)* **"AI" names survive outside the ledger.** After BD-13 the ledger is neutral, but the capability id `ai.actions` (until SQ-17 is ruled at slice 5), the `AiActionType` declarations, `runAiAction`, this requirement's title and several docs still say "AI". That is correct for the AI half; it becomes debt only where it names the **pool**. Owner-facing text is covered by BD-15 | Informational; the pool-level names are reviewed at slices 5, 6 and 7. *(2026-09-30: SQ-17 ruled — the capability id becomes `credits.allowance` in slice 5. SA recorded one remaining pool-level name as naming debt N-12: the capability keeps `category: 'ai_chat'` until slice 6 decides the owner wording.)* |
| **KI-14** | *(added 2026-09-29, N-10)* **A plain filter on `service` silently drops adjustments.** Because adjustment rows carry a NULL `service` and inherit it from the charge they adjust, any query written as `WHERE service = 'ai'` or `GROUP BY service` under-counts or mis-buckets them. Every service-aware read must use the effective service | Known by design (SQ-18 ruling); guarded by slices 4 and 7's guardrails and by AC-34 |
| **KI-15** | *(added 2026-09-30, D3)* **The allowance is a generous ceiling, so it rarely binds.** On the benchmark, recurring use was ~0.2–0.3% of the derived allowances (~330–540× headroom). Consequences, accepted with D3: the allowance will not drive boost purchases at that usage; the 80% / 100% warnings (slice 8) will almost never fire; and the practical guards against runaway spend are the per-action caps (daily image cap, chat daily budget, slice 12's stranger cap and any chat / image cap it adds). Images are the one action that can approach the ceiling (79 images exhaust Essentials).
