**Reviewed by SA — 2026-09-28**, against worktree `feature/business-os-credit-deduction` @ 7120adee (main 7ba40a56).
**Status:** 🔄 **APPROVED WITH CONDITIONS**

BD-1 (option B) is a fixed input and is not reopened here. Everything below is reviewed within it.

### Verdict in one paragraph

The shape is right. The grain is `runAiAction`, the ledger is new and belongs to Business OS, Pilot Credits are left alone, the audit queue is not the billing record, and the layer ships in `shadow` behind the existing mode. All of that is architecturally sound, and it matches the entitlements SA decisions (T-5, T-6, T-9, WC-18). **Three findings block later slices, not D-0.** The ledger's uniqueness key is wrong for today's code (SA-B1). The fail-closed rule contradicts an approved SA decision and the code that already ships (SA-B2). And "no call-site changes" understates D-3 (SA-B3). **D-0 may go to a Dev workplan now** under the scoping below. The requirement must absorb SA-B1 to SA-B3 before the D-1 and D-3 workplans are written.

### Verified as-built (spot checks)

| Claim | Result |
|---|---|
| A-7 `runAiAction` / 16 action types | ✅ `aiActionAudit.ts:38-54`, `:263-293`. **It is wired at 16 call sites.** The file header still says "NOT WIRED YET … nothing calls `runAiAction` in production", which is stale (SA-S12) |
| A-10 `ALWAYS_SUFFICIENT` | ✅ `balance.ts:45-49`. `EntitlementService.check` consults it only for metered capabilities, and **fails open on a balance error** (`EntitlementService.ts:178-184`) |
| A-3 unpriced → $0 | ✅ `pricing.ts:267-269` (sync) and `:229-230` (async). Image: `GeneratedImageService.ts:117`. **But A-3 overstates the risk.** Layer 2 already refuses an unpriced or zero-priced model at settings refill (`modelSettings.ts:36-38`, `checkTokenModel` `:304-305`, `checkImageModel` `:314-327`, DEC-7). The residual holes are narrower (SA-S2) |
| A-9 allowances | ⚠️ Partly stale. There are **two** tiers (`basic` 500, `pro` 2,000 — `tierMatrix.ts:127-129`, `:196`, **"the only one the user has decided"**). Champion now **reads the pro row** (`cohorts.ts:100`), and trial is a one-off `total: 250` (`cohorts.ts:138`). There is no 1,000 and no "four plans" (SA-S11) |
| A-11 / SQ-10 F-28 | ✅ The route never queried `user_subscriptions`. See the SQ-10 ruling |
| A-17 image range | ⚠️ The lower bound is $0.011 (`gpt-image-1:1024x1024:low`, `SystemConfigRepository.ts:69`), not $0.016 |
| `period_anchor` (T-6) | Present in `lib/business-os/entitlements/account.ts`, `BusinessOsAccountPlanRepository.ts` and `supabase/migrations/20261005_business_os_entitlements.sql`. **Unverified against live** |
| Logging debt | `pricing.ts`, `usageScope.ts`, `baseProvider.ts`, `openaiProvider.ts`, `GeneratedImageService.ts` and `aiActionAudit.ts` have **0** `console.*` calls. `anthropicProvider.ts` has 7 and `kimiProvider.ts` has 2, so D-0 must not need to touch them |

### SQ rulings

All twelve are **TECHNICAL**. The reasons are summarised here; the one-line rulings are also appended inline in §11.

| SQ | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| **SQ-1** Ledger | A new Business OS table with one row per charged action, **keyed by a server-minted `action_id`** (SA-B1). It carries: `user_id` (FK `auth.users`), `group_id` (indexed, not unique), area, action type, trigger, outcome, `cost_usd`, `credits`, credit-value version, fallback flag, tokens, `period_start` and `created_at`. Reconciliations (FR-12d) are **separate adjustment rows** that point at the original. It also needs a **per-`(user_id, period_start)` totals row**, updated in the same transaction, so the pre-check is O(1). Grants (D-4) get their own table with the T-9(2) fields | `token_usage` is per call and holds no credits, grants, periods or reconciliations. T-6 already specifies usage "stored per `(account, period_start)`". Immutable action rows plus a maintained total give both the diary and a cheap balance |
| **SQ-2** Idempotency | The grouping-id constraint is **not** sufficient, and it is also **wrong**. Each `runAiAction` invocation mints one `action_id`, and the write is `ON CONFLICT (action_id) DO NOTHING`. A retry that made new calls is a new invocation, so it gets a new row. **Nothing is ever updated in place** | A charge is written once per invocation, in-process. The only duplicate risk is the write itself being retried, which `action_id` covers. See SA-B1 for why the grouping id cannot be the key |
| **SQ-3** Write path | A **direct write** at the end of `runAiAction`, through a repository and one RPC. It is awaited with a short time budget (the workplan proposes the figure, ≤ 2 s) and never throws or rethrows. It is a failure only when it logs `error` with account, area, action type, grouping id and action id. **No outbox and no §8.1 drain** | The data is in hand at that point, and the write lands after seconds of LLM work, so a few tens of ms is immaterial. Fire-and-forget is not an option on serverless: a frozen function loses it, exactly as it does for Layer 3 KI-B. `token_usage` is already an independent durable record of every call, so a reconciliation (SA-S9) detects any lost write without building a second queue. Decide on a backfill once D-1 has measured the loss rate |
| **SQ-4** Reserve vs check | **Check-then-charge.** The pre-check asks "is remaining > 0?" (allowance + live grants − consumed). There is no reservation. Overspend is recorded. It draws from grants when there are any, is shown as 0 remaining (never negative), and is **not** carried into the next period | Under option B the price cannot be known in advance, so a reservation would reintroduce the price list BD-1 rejected. The overspend is bounded by the actions in flight, and the worst single action (a high-quality image, ≈ $0.25) is known. Carrying no debt mirrors no roll-over (B-6) |
| **SQ-5** Cache | The **allowance** may come from the 30 s snapshot. **Consumption is always read fresh** (from the totals row) | Already decided: entitlements T-5, "metered counters are never cached". The stale-snapshot rule for `owner`/`mixed` capabilities (`EntitlementService.ts:150-162`) still applies |
| **SQ-6** Period | **Already decided (T-6):** anniversary periods from `period_anchor`. The diary defaults to the current period | SA ruled it on 2026-09-19 (entitlements §21.1). It matches Stripe's billing cycle in Slice 4 and needs no reset cron. The requirement's "still undecided" is incorrect and should be amended |
| **SQ-7** Credit value | **In the entitlements config (code)**, versioned with the matrix version. Each version carries its value, derivation and date. Ledger rows record the version | FR-3 and FR-37 tie it to the allowances derived from it. An operator-editable row would silently re-price the product with no release and no review. It also means AC-2's "audit entry" is the versioned config record plus its PR, not an `audit_trail` row (SA-S6) |
| **SQ-8** Precision | `cost_usd` is summed from the **raw per-call `costUsd`** and stored as `numeric` with 10 decimal places. `credits` is `numeric` with 6. **Never** derive cost from the audit's `estimatedCostUsd`, which is rounded to a micro-dollar (`aiActionAudit.ts:199`): a plan-cache lookup embedding costs ~1e-7 USD and would round to 0. Round only at display. Used and remaining come from unrounded sums. Diary lines are rounded individually, and the period total is shown once from the unrounded sum, with a one-line footnote ("lines are rounded; the total is exact") | Summing rounded lines cannot be made to equal the rounded sum in general. The honest rule is one exact total and a stated footnote. Whether lines show whole credits or one decimal depends on the credit value D-1b picks: at ≈ $0.001, a p50 chat turn is ≈ 0.6 credit (see Note N-8) |
| **SQ-9** Fallback | "Conservative" means the **highest rate in the in-code price table for the same provider and the same kind** (text, embedding, image), **computed** from the table rather than hand-typed. It lives in the pricing module with its derivation, is flagged on the row and logged at `error`. SA signs it off at code review | After D-0, this path is reachable only through a defect: Layer 2 refuses unpriced models, and the D-0 test covers every code default. So the rule should never lose money and never go stale; it does not need to be tuned. A computed maximum cannot drift behind the table |
| **SQ-10** F-28 | **F-28 is a false positive, so TK-6 is already closed on the read side.** `git log -S user_subscriptions -- app/api/business-os/usage/route.ts` returns one commit (ea35c79d), and in it the string arrives inside the explanatory comment at `:214`. It was never a query. The route does read the agent-platform **rate keys** (`pilot_credit_cost_usd` `:122/:129`; `tokens_per_pilot_credit` in `usageSummary.ts:195`), and FR-36 removes those in D-2 | BA to correct F-28, TK-6 and H-10 in `BUSINESS_OS_TIER_BILLING_REUSE_PLAN.md` (re-scope TK-6 to "retire the rate-key reads, delivered by D-2"). The other Business OS mentions (`accountDeletionPolicy.ts:94-107`, `purge/descriptors.ts:337-340`) are data-lifecycle policy, not billing reads |
| **SQ-11** Sequencing | **Accepted**, with three conditions: (1) the D-1 ledger write is proven unable to throw into, delay beyond its time budget, or change the result of any action (the WC-21 standard); (2) **D-2 is hard-gated on D-1b** (SA-S10); (3) in `shadow`, the 80/100% warnings are recorded and logged but **not delivered to owners** until D-1b's allowances ship | Shadow refuses nothing, so metering first produces the measurement S-5 needs anyway. But D-1 puts a DB write in the path of every production AI action, so "no behaviour change" has to be proven, not assumed |
| **SQ-12** FR-12 scope; audience home | **No full pricing rework** (no cache warming, refresh or cached-input rates). The boundary is SA-S2 and SA-S3. The **declared list keyed by `AiActionType`** is the right home for audience, setup flag, chargeable, diary label and fallback status. Audience is uniform per type today: `lead_reply_recommendation` is client-facing and every other type is owner-facing. This supersedes WC-18's per-call-name inventory **for these attributes only**, and `BOS_LLM_CALLS` stays the attribution and pricing inventory. `atLimit: 'by_call_site_audience'` then resolves through that list | Under option B, cost is per call but behaviour at the limit is per action, so each fact lives at its own grain. WC-18's "embedding calls do not count" is also superseded, because their cost is charged (N-2) |

### Business question for the user (BQ-1)

This is the only item that needs the product owner. It comes out of SA-B2, not out of an SQ.

> **When our record of how much AI a business has used is briefly unreachable (a database hiccup, usually seconds to minutes), what should the owner's own AI assistant do?**
> - **A. Pause it.** We never give away AI we can't count, but every owner loses their assistant during any hiccup, including owners with plenty of credit left. (This is what BD-5 currently says.)
> - **B. Keep it working, and charge afterwards.** Nobody is interrupted. The usage is still recorded when the database recovers, so almost nothing is given away. An owner who had already run out could get a few extra answers during the hiccup. (This is what SA decided for entitlements before option B, and what the code does today.)
> - **C. Keep it working for owners we last saw with credit left, and pause only owners we last saw at zero.** It behaves like B for almost everyone and like A for the few who were already out.
>
> **SA recommendation: C.** Under option B every action is charged after it runs anyway, so a hiccup costs us almost nothing. Pausing every owner's assistant for a hiccup that is our fault is the worse customer experience. Client-facing work keeps running under all three options.

> ✅ **DECIDED 2026-09-28 by the user: option C.** When the balance cannot be read, the owner's own AI keeps working unless the account was last seen at zero; then it gets the retryable `entitlement_unavailable` outcome (SA-B2), never "out of credits". SA-B2 is to be folded in on this basis before the D-3 workplan.

**Heads-up, not a question yet:** D-1b will need one business input from the user, **the AI cost we are willing to carry per plan per month**. It replaces the 500 / 2,000 the user already decided as action counts (SA-S11). That is asked when D-1's numbers exist, not now.

### Findings

#### Blocking (must be folded into the requirement before the named slice's workplan; none blocks D-0)

| # | Finding | Blocks |
|---|---|---|
| **SA-B1** | **The grouping id is not one-per-action, so it cannot be the ledger's unique key.** (a) `onboarding_turn` uses `currentState.attributionGroupId` (`app/api/onboarding/chat/route.ts:224`), which is minted **once per conversation** and persisted (`OnboardingConversationManager.ts:2159`, `:2172-2177`). A unique constraint would therefore charge the first onboarding turn and silently drop every later one. (b) `chat_turn` uses `turnId = incomingCorrelationId` when it is a UUID (`chat-v4/route.ts:392`), which is **client-supplied**. Under FR-9's "replay writes no second row", a client that resends one header gets **every chat turn free**. **Fix:** make the idempotency key an `action_id` minted inside `runAiAction` per invocation. Keep `group_id` stored and indexed but not unique. Amend FR-9, FR-13, NFR Correctness, AC-8 and AC-28. To keep ledger↔audit joins exact, the D-1 workplan should also add the `action_id` to the audit details (a `schema: 2` bump), because a grouping id can now map to several audit entries | D-1 |
| **SA-B2** | **FR-21 / AC-15 contradict an approved decision and shipped code.** Entitlements **T-3(d)** says "Metered AI pre-check: **fail open**", and `EntitlementService.check` does exactly that (`:178-184`). `ai.actions` is audience `mixed` in the catalog (`catalog.ts:293-305`), so the catalog-driven failure policy (WC-10) cannot tell owner-facing from client-facing on its own. **Fix:** after BQ-1 is answered, state that FR-21 supersedes T-3(d) for this capability. Resolve the audience per action type from the FR-4 list, not from the catalog. Make any refusal on an unreadable balance the retryable `entitlement_unavailable` outcome, **never** "out of credits" and never an upgrade prompt. Update the comment and the catch block in `EntitlementService` in the same change | D-3 |
| **SA-B3** | **FR-17 "No call site changes" is untrue.** No AI call site asks the entitlement resolver anything today: `ENFORCEMENT_POINTS` is empty on purpose (`enforcementPoints.ts`), and no production code calls `EntitlementService.check` for `ai.actions`. `ALWAYS_SUFFICIENT` removed the *interface* migration, not the *wiring*. **Fix:** D-3 adds the pre-check in **one** place, `runAiAction`, before `fn` runs, using the action type → audience list. That needs the account known up front, and chat-v4 is the one site that sets it late (`h.setAccount(user.id)`, `chat-v4/route.ts:433`), so it must be restructured. Register the gate in `ENFORCEMENT_POINTS` (its test enforces the truth). The pre-check must be unable to throw in `shadow` (WC-21) | D-3 |

#### Should-fix

| # | Finding |
|---|---|
| **SA-S1** | **FR-7 / AC-6: a plan-cache hit is not free.** The lookup embedding is itself a paid call inside the turn (`PlanCache.ts:233`, `plan_cache_lookup_embedding`). Restate this as "charged exactly what it spent; a plan-cache hit costs only its lookup embedding (a small non-zero fraction)". The image reuse cache really does make no call, so it is 0 |
| **SA-S2** | **FR-12e / AC-3: "every model an operator can select" cannot be enumerated.** The model is a free-text field, and Layer 2 already refuses unpriced or zero-priced ones at refill (DEC-7). Restate it as a test that every **code default** is priced > 0 in the in-code tables: `BOS_LLM_CALL_POLICY` defaults, `IMAGE_GENERATION_CONFIG_DEFAULTS.model` × every configured size × low/medium/high, and the default embedding model behind the excluded chat calls (input > 0). Cite the DEC-7 gate as the runtime guarantee for DB-selected models. The residual holes are: an excluded call's model, a model priced only in the DB when a cold instance's pricing load failed, and a new call site outside Layer 2 |
| **SA-S3** | **The FR-12 fallback must not change shared pricing return values.** `calculateCostSync`, `calculateCost` and `resolveImagePrice` serve the agent platform too. Instead: an optional price-source signal on `UsageCallRecord` (set by `openaiProvider` for tokens, and from `ImagePriceSource` for images), and the **Business OS charge resolver** applies the conservative price and flags the row. Promoting the existing log level in `pricing.ts` to `error` is acceptable, because it changes the level only and the file is already on Pino. `token_usage` keeps recording what we priced |
| **SA-S4** | **FR-23 / AC-17: "a setup action that *would* exhaust" cannot be known under option B.** State the estimate's source: the action type's D-1-measured p90 (or the owner's own history once there is enough). It is used for the warning only and is never the charge |
| **SA-S5** | **FR-26: the diary must read the ledger, not "the same per-action record as the audit entry".** The audit is lossy (A-8), and a diary that disagreed with the balance would break FR-26's reconciliation rule |
| **SA-S6** | **AC-2:** the credit value lives in code config (SQ-7), so "writes one audit entry" becomes: the version record carries value, before/after, date and derivation; the ledger rows carry the version; and the process logs the active version at start-up |
| **SA-S7** | **Strangers can spend an owner's allowance.** `lead_reply_recommendation` runs on a public enquiry (`trigger: 'external'`, `LeadAlertService.ts:344-345`). Under `enforce`, a form-spammer could exhaust the allowance and pause the owner's own AI. Before enforcement (D-3), add a per-account daily cap on charged `external` actions, like the image cap. Past the cap, the lead reply degrades to template text, which is the client-facing path that exists anyway |
| **SA-S8** | **Standards the new tables must meet (WC-3, WC-4, WC-7, WC-8, WC-9, tenant-isolation):** Business OS-namespaced names with an FK to `auth.users(id)`; RLS owner-SELECT only with no user write policy (the money-table lockdown); repository-only access, with `.eq('user_id', …)` on reads and a documented reason for each `supabaseServer` use; any RPC is `SECURITY INVOKER` if possible, otherwise `REVOKE EXECUTE … FROM public, anon, authenticated`, with `search_path` pinned; the account id always comes from `runAiAction`'s server-derived spec, never a body; registered in `purge/descriptors.ts` (never-purged) and `accountDeletionPolicy.ts` (verdict); grant rows store the actor, reason, `created_at` and `expires_at` themselves, and the admin audit is flushed before responding |
| **SA-S9** | **Scope leakage is uncharged spend.** A call is left out of a usage scope if it carries a different `sessionId` or finishes after the scope closes (`usageScope.ts:108-124`), so it never reaches the ledger. The D-1 report (FR-33) must include a **ledger-vs-`token_usage` reconciliation** per account and period (features `business-os-*`, by `session_id`). The same check catches FR-16's failed writes |
| **SA-S10** | **D-2 must not ship before D-1b.** Otherwise the owner's card shows placeholder allowances converted to credits. In `shadow`, warnings are recorded but not delivered to owners until D-1b ships (SQ-11 condition 3) |
| **SA-S11** | **A-9 / FR-37 correction.** 500 (basic) and 2,000 (pro) were **decided by the user** as action counts (`tierMatrix.ts:127-128`). Champion reads the pro row, and trial is a one-off 250. FR-37 replaces a user decision, so D-1b needs the user's sign-off on the per-plan target cost (the heads-up above). Keep the capability **id** `ai.actions` (admin overrides in the DB reference it) and change only its `unit` and labels |
| **SA-S12** | `aiActionAudit.ts`'s header says "NOT WIRED YET"; it is wired at 16 sites. Fix it in D-0, which touches the file's neighbourhood |

#### Notes

- **N-1** SQ-10: F-28 is closed on the read side. BA to correct the tier-billing plan (F-28, TK-6, H-10, E-3).
- **N-2** WC-18's "embedding calls do not count" was written for a flat count. Under option B their (tiny) cost is charged.
- **N-3** A failed provider call is recorded at $0 (`baseProvider.ts:166-176`). "Charged what was spent" therefore means *what we measured*: a failure the provider billed anyway (for example, a timeout after generation) is not charged. Accept it and add it to §14 as a KI.
- **N-4** Provider-cached input tokens are charged at the full input rate (`cachedInputTokens` is recorded but not priced). The owner is slightly over-charged, which is conservative. Add it to §14 as a KI; it is already out of scope in §13.
- **N-5** `calculateCostSync` never re-checks the 1 h pricing TTL, so a price can be stale but is never $0 once loaded. Acceptable.
- **N-6** A-17's lower image bound is $0.011.
- **N-7** No `durable-queue-drain` work is needed while nothing drains a table. If a backfill job is ever added after SA-S9's measurement, it follows §8.1.
- **N-8** For D-1b: pick a credit value that makes a typical chat turn at least 1 whole credit. At ≈ $0.001 a p50 chat turn is ≈ 0.6 credit, so whole-credit display would show "0" or "1" for most of the diary.

### D-0 scoping for the Dev workplan

**D-0 is well-scoped enough to start now**, once the workplan applies SA-S2, SA-S3 and SA-S12. It is pure code and tests: no migration, no DB, no UI, and no runtime behaviour change.

**The workplan must include**

1. **The declared action list** as one typed module in `lib/business-os/llm/`, next to `aiActionAudit.ts`. It is a `Record<AiActionType, …>` with `satisfies`, and for every type it declares: area, audience (`owner` \| `client`), setup flag, chargeable, a diary-label key (en/he/es, following the catalog's label pattern) and template-fallback status (`exists` \| `missing` \| `n/a`). It must be impossible to add a type without an entry. Because `next build` ignores type errors, a **Jest guard test** enforces this, and the file sits inside the `typecheck:bos-llm` gate. The two dormant types (KI-4) are included.
2. **The fallback inventory.** `lead_reply_recommendation` is recorded as `missing`: when the model returns nothing, no reply is queued (`LeadAlertService.ts:361-366`). Record it; **do not build it** (that is D-3 / OI-1).
3. **Price coverage tests** (SA-S2): every code default is priced > 0, and every image default × size × quality resolves > 0. Reference the existing DEC-7 tests rather than duplicating them.
4. **The unpriced signal** (SA-S3): an optional, product-agnostic field on `UsageCallRecord`, set in `openaiProvider` (token path and image path). Absent means "unknown" and is treated conservatively by the Business OS resolver. `anthropicProvider` and `kimiProvider` are **not touched**, because both carry `console.*` debt.
5. **The conservative-price function and the flag** (SQ-9) as a **pure** Business OS function that nothing calls yet (D-1 wires it), with unit tests: a priced call passes through, an unpriced text call is charged the class maximum and flagged, an unpriced image likewise, and the `error` log names provider, model, area, action type and grouping id.
6. Optionally, raise the unpriced log level in `pricing.ts` from `warn` to `error`. This is level-only.
7. Fix the stale header in `aiActionAudit.ts` (SA-S12).

> **BA note (2026-09-28):** item 2 above is **corrected by SA's C-1** (§14): `lead_reply_recommendation` already falls back to `pickFallbackCandidate` on every failure, so slice 1 records it as `exists`. Item 4's "treated conservatively" is refined by SQ-13 (2): an absent signal is re-checked against the price table, not assumed unpriced. The SA text above is unchanged.

**The workplan must avoid**

- Changing what `calculateCost`, `calculateCostSync` or `resolveImagePrice` **return**, or changing what `token_usage` records.
- Hand-typed fallback prices: they are computed from the table.
- Putting audience into the entitlements catalog, or renaming the `ai.actions` id.
- Any change to `runAiAction`'s behaviour, the audit `details` schema, `balance.ts`, `EntitlementService`, `ENFORCEMENT_POINTS`, any call site, or any file under `CreditService` / `pricingConfig`.
- A migration. The ledger is D-1, and its shape follows SA-B1.

**D-0 exit criteria:** `npm test` on the touched suites, `typecheck:bos-llm` and `check:bos-llm-literals` are all green. A grep shows no call site changed, and the SA code review confirms there is no runtime behaviour change.

SQ-17 and SQ-19 are ruled below; both are TECHNICAL. One business question remains, BD-16, and it is already the BA's.

#### Live read-only check (2026-09-30, SELECTs only, service role from the main checkout)

| Table | Finding |
|---|---|
| `business_os_entitlement_overrides` | **0 rows in total** — none on `ai.actions`, active or ended |
| `business_os_entitlement_shadow_events` | 16 rows in total, **0 on `ai.actions`** (nothing calls `check()` on it; `ENFORCEMENT_POINTS` has no entry for it) |
| `business_os_account_plans` | 8 rows, **all `cohort = champion`, `tier = NULL`, `plan_version = 0`** — no account holds a tier, so no plan row carries a matrix version |
| SQL (migrations, RPCs, checkers) | No reference to `ai.actions` (the audit `entity_type 'ai_action'` is a different thing — see guardrail G-9) |

**Override finding.** There are **no** `add_override` (or any) rows on `ai.actions`, so the BA's concern — an override set in action counts silently meaning credits — has **nothing to act on today**. It can become true before slice 5 ships: `adminOps` accepts `add_override` on `ai.actions` right now. Condition C-3 below makes the Dev re-run the count before merge.

#### SQ-17 ruling — rename the capability id now

**Ruled: rename `ai.actions` → `credits.allowance`, with unit `'credit'` and neutral labels, in slice 5. No transitional alias.** This supersedes SA-S11's "keep the id".

Why: SA-S11 kept the id **only** because stored overrides reference it. Measured today, none do, no shadow event does, no plan row does, and no SQL does. Every reader is TypeScript inside `lib/business-os/entitlements/**` plus its tests and two admin fixtures, so `TierRow` / `CohortExplicitValues` (mapped types over the catalog) turn every missed reference into a compile error. Slice 11 (admin grant / reduce) is where overrides on this id start to be written in volume, and slice 6 is its first owner-facing reader; after either, a rename becomes a data migration. **This is the cheapest the rename will ever be**, and BD-12 / BD-15 make the old name wrong (it is one pool for every service, not "AI actions").

| Reader | What changes |
|---|---|
| `config/catalog.ts` | Entry re-keyed to `credits.allowance`; `shape: { kind: 'metered', unit: 'credit', period: 'month' }`; labels a working neutral "Credits" (en) / "קרדיטים" (he) / "Créditos" (es) — final owner wording stays with slices 6 / 7 (BD-15); `note` rewritten (the B-4 "flat count of actions" is superseded by BD-1). `audience: 'mixed'`, `atLimit: 'by_call_site_audience'`, `sellableAsAddon: false`, `lifecycle: 'available'` unchanged. `category: 'ai_chat'` is **kept** in slice 5 (a new category needs a customer heading and three translations — slice 6's wording decision); recorded as naming debt N-12 |
| `types.ts`, `schema.ts` | Metered unit union / `z.enum` becomes `'credit' \| 'sms'`. `'ai_action'` is **removed**, not kept alongside — nothing else uses it |
| `config/tierMatrix.ts`, `config/cohorts.ts` | New key and new values (below). `CHAMPION_VALUES` keeps **reading** the `pro` row (structural parity), it is not given a literal |
| `adminPlansView.ts` (+ `app/admin/business-os-tiers` `types.ts`, `PlanCard.tsx`) | Field `aiActions` → `credits`; the card's "AI actions" label → "Credits" |
| `customerPlanView.ts`, `planPresentation.ts` | Read the new id; the admin "or when the AI actions run out" text and the owner copy `plan.ends_on_or_actions` (en / es / he in `LanguageContext.tsx`) say **credits**. Keeping the i18n **key** name is acceptable; renaming it to `plan.ends_on_or_credits` is preferred if the Dev is already in the three locale blocks — either way `planCopy.i18n.test.ts` is updated in the same change |
| Overrides / `adminOps` | No code change: `add_override` validates `capability` against the catalog keys, so the new id is accepted and the old one refused automatically. No data migration (0 rows) — subject to C-3 |
| Shadow events | No change and no migration (0 rows on the old id; nothing records against it) |
| Snapshot | `npm run entitlements:snapshot` regenerates it. **Note for the code review:** `findDrift` skips a snapshot capability that is no longer in the catalog (`snapshot.ts`, "the catalog test owns that") and never looks at capabilities absent from the snapshot, so **the drift guard is blind to this one change**. SA will read the snapshot diff by hand: the old key gone from both tiers, the new key at 19,750 / 32,250, nothing else moved |
| `removals` | **No `removals` entry.** A rename is not a removal: no customer loses anything, both new values are raises, and a `removals` entry naming an id that is no longer in the catalog would describe a capability that does not exist. The rename is recorded in the `BUSINESS_OS_ENTITLEMENTS.md` Change History and the tier-matrix header instead |
| Tests and fixtures | `catalog.invariant.test.ts` (expected id list and the `atLimit` assertion), `productionConfig`, `resolver`, `decide`, `entitlementService`, `customerPlanView`, `adminOps`, `notBuiltCannotBeAllocated`, `tierMatrix.invariant`, `tierMatrix.snapshot`, `__fixtures__/exampleTierMatrix.ts`, `app/admin/business-os-tiers/__tests__/page.render.test.tsx` and its two `recordedAccountBody*.json` fixtures |
| Entitlements invariants CI check | The capability-id literal guard in `enforcementPoints.test.ts` scans product code outside the module; no product file outside the module needs the new id. `npm run test:bos-entitlements` must be green on the final diff |
| Living docs and the skill | `BUSINESS_OS_ENTITLEMENTS.md`, the pricing doc (§1, §6.3 step 3 "or its successor"), and `.claude/skills/business-os-entitlements/SKILL.md` (it says paid tiers differ only by `chat.*` and `ai.actions`). **Historical** workplans and requirements are **not** edited |

#### SQ-19 ruling — bump the matrix to version 2

**Ruled: yes. `TIER_MATRIX.version` 1 → 2, `removals` stays empty, credit value version 1 carries `matrixVersion: 2`.** A bump with no removal is permitted and has no side effect:

- **Snapshot test:** `findDrift` reports a raise as `changed` (accepted by refreshing the snapshot) whatever the version; `bumped` only matters for a `lowered` value. The regenerated snapshot records `matrixVersion: 2`.
- **`creditValue.test.ts`:** `matrixVersion` must be non-decreasing and `≤ TIER_MATRIX.version` — v0 (1) then v1 (2) with the matrix at 2 passes.
- **Plan rows / grandfathering:** `plan_version` is written only when an admin assigns a **tier** (`adminOps.ts:412`, set to the current matrix version). No row holds a tier today (all 8 are champion, `plan_version = 0`), so nothing is re-stamped; the migration's `plan_version > 0` check for tier rows is unaffected. The resolver uses `planVersion` **only** in the tier grandfathering layer, which compares it with `removals[].version` — there are none, so no account's resolution changes. A later removal recorded at version ≥ 3 grandfathers v1 and v2 tier accounts alike, which is correct.
- **Cohorts:** a cohort account's effective version falls back to the matrix version, but that value is read only inside the tier-only grandfathering branch. No effect.
- **Shadow events:** have no version column. No effect.
- **Display:** the admin Tiers page shows `matrixVersion` (`adminPlansView.ts:219`) — it will read 2, which is the point.

The bump is also the honest record of the rename: matrix v2 is the first matrix whose allowance is in credits.

#### The credit value, the formula and where the markup lives

- **Version 1 appended, version 0 untouched — confirmed as the right shape.** `{ version: 1, usdPerCredit: 0.001, status: 'derived', matrixVersion: 2, decidedOn: '2026-09-30', derivation: <names D1–D3, points at the pricing doc> }`, appended to both `CREDIT_VALUE_HISTORY` and `creditValue.history.json` in the same commit; v0 byte-for-byte unchanged. The ledger needs no migration: `credit_value_version` is an unconstrained non-negative integer, and `chargeResolver.ts` reads `currentCreditValue()`, so every charge after deploy records version 1 at the same $0.001 — no recorded charge moves (FR-3).
- **Formula — checked.** (fee × 0.50 ÷ 2) ÷ $0.001: $79 → 19,750; $129 → 32,250. Both fit the `z.number().int().nonnegative()` metered value schema.
- **Markup in the allowance, not the charge — endorsed.** Keeping the ledger an unmarked record of cost is what makes the slice 4b leak check (ledger vs `token_usage`) and the cost report meaningful; a marked-up credit would make both disagree by design. Guardrail G-4.
- **N-8 is met.** My N-8 estimate (~0.6 credit) used ChatBudget's per-call p50; a turn is several calls. The measured ~$0.002 per turn (≈ 2 credits) is the right figure, and the pricing doc labels the reconciliation as inference, which is correct.

#### Pricing doc — a good single source

Meets the doc standards (header, Last Updated, Overview, ToC, `---` separators, Change History at the end), keeps the numbers, the decisions, the data and the revisit procedure in one place, and records only account-level aggregates under "Benchmark account A / B" — no ids, emails or names. Two small fixes, in the slice 5 PR: **P-1** §4.7 caveat 2 says "her AI actions" of account B — make it "its", so the doc does not hint at who the account belongs to; **P-2** after SQ-17, §1's "after slice 5 ships" table, the SQ-17 open item and §6.3 step 3 name `credits.allowance`, and OI-9's §1 / revision-log update lands in the same PR (a revision-log row for the rename and the trial total).

#### BD-16 — confirmed as the user's decision; constraints SA sees

The BA's two options are correctly stated: **about 2,000 credits** (≈ $2 of cost per trial at most; ~6× the worst measured setup month of ~347) or **a 14-day share of Essentials, about 9,200** (≈ $9.20 at most). Constraints, whichever the user picks:

1. It is a **one-off total** (`{ total: n }`), never `perMonth`; running out **ends the trial** (FR-18, FR-27), and `customerPlanView` keys the "or when the credits run out" wording on the `total` shape — so it must stay a `total`.
2. It must be a whole number (the value schema is integer).
3. It must comfortably exceed a real setup (B-12): one generated image alone is ~250 credits, so anything near the old 250 ends a genuine trial on day one.
4. The trial previews Essentials (`base: { tier: 'basic' }`); a total in credits and Essentials' monthly rate are different shapes, and the plan screen deliberately makes no "more / less" claim between them (tested) — so no ordering constraint against 19,750 exists.
5. A tier (and so the trial's base) may never grant a `not_built` capability — unaffected: the credit allowance is `available`, and slice 5 changes no lifecycle.

#### Conditions (for the workplan and the PR)

- **C-1** The rename and the numbers ship in **one PR** with the matrix bump; no intermediate state where the id is renamed but values are placeholders, or vice versa.
- **C-2** `npm run test:bos-entitlements` green on the final diff and reported in the workplan; `npm run entitlements:snapshot` run, not hand-edited; the snapshot diff pasted into the workplan for SA to read (the drift guard is blind to a rename — see SQ-17).
- **C-3** **Before merge**, re-run the read-only count of `business_os_entitlement_overrides` rows on `ai.actions` (any state) and record it in the workplan. If it is **not 0**, stop and return to SA: those rows would be silently ignored by the resolver under the new id and their values are action counts, so they need a decided conversion (and the user's say, since it changes what an account gets), not a mechanical rename.
- **C-4** Tests that pin `creditValueVersion` / `credit_value_version` **0** as *the current value* (`chargeResolver.test.ts:144` and the recorder / audit tests built from the resolver) are changed to read `currentCreditValue().version`; tests that use 0 as arbitrary row data may stay.
- **C-5** Merge only after BD-16 is decided; the trial value in `cohorts.ts` is the user's figure, with a pointer to the pricing doc's revision-log row.

#### Guardrails

- **G-1** Config, copy and docs only — no change to the charge path, the ledger schema, any RPC or any migration.
- **G-2** Version 0 of the credit value and its JSON entry are not edited; $0.001 is not changed.
- **G-3** Nothing is lowered; no `removals` entry; matrix `version: 2`.
- **G-4** No markup on the charge; the markup lives in allowance size only (D1).
- **G-5** No enforcement, no new `check()` / `decide()` caller, no new `ENFORCEMENT_POINTS` entry, no new importer of the module from outside it (if one appears, the `business-os-entitlements` skill's registration rules apply).
- **G-6** No lifecycle flip; no tier grants a `not_built` capability.
- **G-7** `CHAMPION_VALUES` reads the `pro` row; no literal 32,250 anywhere in config except `pro`.
- **G-8** Rationale lives in the pricing doc; code gets one pointer line per file, not the reasoning.
- **G-9** **Do not touch the audit trail's `entity_type 'ai_action'`**, `AiActionType`, `runAiAction` or anything named "AI action" outside the entitlements capability — that is the audit / charge vocabulary for an AI action, unrelated to the plan allowance, and an RLS policy (`20260930_audit_trail_owner_policy_hides_ai_actions.sql`) keys on it.
- **G-10** Owner-facing wording is a working "Credits" only; the final label, a neutral category heading, and the usage card are slices 6 / 7 (BD-15).

#### Sizing

**One slice, about two days, one PR, no migration.** Roughly: half a day for config + credit value v1 + matrix bump; half to one day for the rename through types, schema, views, admin card, i18n copy and ~14 test / fixture files (the compiler finds them); half a day for the entitlements doc, the pricing doc, the skill, the snapshot and the checks. No split is needed.

**Reviewed by SA, 2026-09-30**, against the BA's [Slice 6 scoping](#slice-6-scoping-ba-2026-09-30), §13 D-a to D-g, AC-37, SQ-20 to SQ-28, KI-18 and OI-10, on branch `feature/business-os-credit-deduction-slice-6` (off `origin/main` `d5a7a93a`). Read as-built: `components/business-os/UsageCard.tsx`, `app/api/business-os/usage/route.ts` and its two tests, `lib/business-os/usage/usageSummary.ts` and its admin consumers (`llmUsageReport.ts`, `llmUsageVerification.ts` Check 5, `CheckPanels.tsx`, `boundaries.static.test.ts`), `supabase/migrations/20261015_business_os_credit_charges.sql` (tables, grants, `business_os_credit_period_start`, `business_os_record_credit_charge`), `BusinessOsCreditLedgerReadRepository`, `effectiveFields.ts`, `BusinessOsAccountPlanRepository`, `adminOps.ts` (anchor resets), `EntitlementService`, `resolver.ts` (`withheldValue`), `balance.ts`, `customerPlanView.ts`, `planOfferView.ts`, the `my-plan` route, the slice 5 workplan T6c list, and the `business-os-entitlements`, `new-api-route`, `new-repository`, `tenant-isolation-guard` and `bos-llm-call-standards` skills. No live query was needed. Review only; no code.

**Status: ✅ APPROVED WITH CONDITIONS.** The owner-facing design (§1 of the scoping) is sound and every guardrail from the slice 6 table is kept. Two BA premises are corrected by the as-built code (C-S6-1, C-S6-2 below), which makes the read path simpler, not harder. All nine SQs are TECHNICAL and ruled here. One genuine business question is added (**D-h**). **The slice 6 Dev workplan may start once the user has answered D-a to D-g** (and D-h, or the BA folds D-h into D-b), after the BA folds in the conditions listed at the end.

| # | Fact | Where |
|---|---|---|
| F-1 | **The totals row already carries the trigger split.** `business_os_credit_totals` has `credits_owner`, `credits_scheduled`, `credits_external` and `credits_adjustment`, written in the same transaction as the charge, and a CHECK that `credits_total` = the four summed. §3 item 1 and SQ-22 say "the totals row has one figure per account per period and no trigger" — incorrect | migration `20261015` `:55-76`, `:230-258` |
| F-2 | **No adjustment writer exists yet.** Nothing inserts `kind = 'adjustment'`; `credits_adjustment` is always 0 today. Readers (`creditReport.ts`, `creditLeakCheck.ts`) already handle adjustment rows | grep of `lib`, `app`, `supabase` |
| F-3 | **Owners hold column grants** on `business_os_credit_charges` (all but `cost_usd`, `is_fallback_priced`) and on `business_os_credit_totals` (all but `cost_usd_total`, `fallback_priced_count`), with owner-SELECT RLS policies on both | `20261015` `:110-132` |

Its header's "a later owner reader can pass the RLS client" is not true for any method it has | `BusinessOsCreditLedgerReadRepository.ts:126-132` |

#### Corrections to the scoping

- **C-S6-1 (SQ-22's premise).** The split does not need a Node sum over the period's charge rows: the totals row already holds it (F-1). See the SQ-22 ruling.
- **C-S6-2 (SQ-21's premise).** "A method on `BusinessOsCreditLedgerReadRepository` called with the owner's RLS client" cannot work as the repository stands (F-4). See the SQ-21 ruling.

#### SQ rulings

| SQ | Ruling (TECHNICAL) |
|---|---|
| **SQ-20** — the period and the trial total | **The period key comes from the database function, never from a Node port.** One server-only module (suggested `lib/business-os/credits/creditPeriod.ts`) resolves the caller's current period: (1) reads the anchor through a new narrow method on `BusinessOsAccountPlanRepository` (`user_id, period_anchor` only; service role, which that repository already is by design; **uncached** — the recorder reads the live anchor, so the card must too, or for up to 30 s after a plan change it would read the old period); (2) gets `period_start` by calling `business_os_credit_period_start(anchor, now)` through a read-only repository RPC wrapper on the service role (F-5; the function is pure and reads no table, so the bypass exposes nothing; document it in code per CLAUDE.md); (3) with **no plan row**, uses the recorder's `calendar_month` rule (F-6), pinned by a source test that reads that expression from the migration, so a change to the recorder breaks the test. **Pass the anchor and `period_start` as the strings PostgREST returned, never through a JS `Date`** — they are microsecond `timestamptz` values and the totals row is matched with `.eq('period_start', …)`; a millisecond-truncated value matches nothing and the card would silently read 0 (the SA CR-B1 trap). **The reset date** (display only, never a key or a filter) may be computed in Node as anchor + (k + 1) months in **UTC**, clamping the day to the target month's last day (Postgres `+ interval 'n months'` semantics), counted **from the anchor**, never by adding a month to the period start (which drifts after a clamped month); fixture tests for anchors on the 28th–31st, 29 February and a year wrap. Format it in the business's timezone from `user_preferences.timezone` (CLAUDE.md § Currency & Timezone; never `business_profiles.timezone`); if the dashboard does not already hold it, the server may send the formatted day and month as facts. **The trial total** = the sum of the account's totals rows with `period_start >= period_anchor` (the anchor string, per F-7), not "since `trial_started_at`" (the invite-signup path does not set `trial_started_at`, and a microsecond gap between the two would drop the first period). The workplan confirms that every path that puts an account on the trial (invite signup, `set_cohort`, `ensurePlanRow`, the `20261005` backfill) leaves the anchor at the trial's start. **No reset date for the trial or for the no-plan-row state** |
| **SQ-21** — the owner's read path | **The owner's RLS client** (`createAuthenticatedServerClient`), as the BA suggests, through a **new, separate read-only repository** (suggested `BusinessOsCreditOwnerReadRepository`), not new methods on the admin one (F-4): its constructor **requires** a client (no service-role default, so it cannot be built as a bypass by accident); it selects only owner-granted columns — totals: `period_start, credits_total, credits_owner, credits_scheduled, credits_external, credits_adjustment`; charges (adjustment attribution only): `kind, action_id, adjusts_action_id, credits, triggered_by, period_start` — as exported constants, with a test that each is a subset of the `authenticated` GRANT lines in migration `20261015` (parsed, not retyped); every method takes the account id and adds `.eq('user_id', …)` on top of RLS (rule 4); the same "no write verbs in the source" test as its sibling; `maybeSingle()` with read errors returned, never turned into "nothing used". Correct the sibling's header sentence (F-4) in the same change. **No service-role read of ledger rows is added to an owner route.** The only service-role calls on this route are the ones that already exist or read no tenant data: the entitlements snapshot (existing), the anchor (F-5) and the pure period function |
| **SQ-22** — the "by you / automatic" split | **From the totals row** (F-1): by you = `credits_owner`, automatic = `credits_scheduled + credits_external`, used = `credits_total`. **No migration, no per-row sum.** Only when `credits_adjustment ≠ 0` does the reader load that period's adjustment rows and their originals (owner RLS client, same account) and attribute each adjustment by its **effective trigger** through `effectiveFields.ts` (N-10); an adjustment whose original cannot be read is attributed to neither part and logged at `warn`, and "used" still comes from `credits_total`. No writer exists yet (F-2), so this branch is small and fixture-tested only — but it is built now so the first adjustment writer cannot silently skew the split. The CHECK makes the parts add up exactly; display rounding (D-c) is a separate step (below). The BA's "if they disagree, the totals row wins and warn" is kept only for the adjustment branch |
| **SQ-23** — `usageSummary.ts` and Check 5 | **As the BA suggests.** `usageSummary.ts` stays for the admin report only. Reword its header, the route-era comments that point at it, the `TokenUsageRepository.ts:7` consumer note, and Check 5's **visible** title and description to "token usage by feature" with no claim to match the owner card. Renaming the identifiers (`UsageCardCheck`, `evaluateUsageCardCheck`, `usageCard`) is **optional** — recorded as naming debt if left. Delete `buildCardBreakdown` / `CardBreakdownLine` and their tests if the grep confirms no other consumer (F-10). No new link between the admin report and the card; the cross-check in scoping §3 item 9 is a test, not a runtime link |
| **SQ-24** — refresh trigger (a) | **Approved as a new, named client pattern (rule 7)**, in one module (suggested `lib/business-os/client/creditUsageSignal.ts`): `notifyCreditUsageChanged()` and `onCreditUsageChanged(handler) → unsubscribe`, over a `window` `CustomEvent` whose name is **one exported constant** (the `currencyChanged` precedent, F-14, but typed and in one place). **No payload** — the card always re-reads; it never trusts figures from an event. Raised **once per owner-started AI action, after it completes** (after a streamed chat answer has finished, not when it starts), **whether it succeeded or failed** (a failed action can be charged). No `BroadcastChannel` (other tabs are covered by trigger b) and no React context (the surfaces are spread across `LiveDashboard.tsx`). A raise with no card mounted is a no-op. The card coalesces with an in-flight flag (one request at a time; a signal that arrives during a request causes exactly one re-read after it) — **no `setTimeout` or `setInterval`**, which AC-33's source guard also forbids in the card and in the signal module. The workplan lists every owner AI surface on the card's page that raises it. Known lag, for QA: the charge write is awaited with a short time budget (SQ-3), so on a timed-out write the card catches up on the next trigger |
| **SQ-25** — the credits category (D-g) | **If the user picks D-g B:** a category `credits`, first in `CATEGORY_PRESENTATION`, its heading key in en / he / es, the category-order test updated, the catalog comment at `catalog.ts:302` updated; if `category` is a typed union, extend it; regenerate the snapshot with `npm run entitlements:snapshot` if the category is in it (never hand-edit); the admin Tiers view groups by category too, so check it. **If D-g C:** the same, with the existing `Platform` heading and no new key. **If D-g A:** close N-12 as accepted debt. Under the `business-os-entitlements` skill; `npm run test:bos-entitlements` green |
| **SQ-26** — internal accounts | **Confirmed: no special case.** Neither the route nor the card may know an account is internal; an internal account shows what its own plan row resolves to, or the no-plan-row state |
| **SQ-27** — room for grants | **A numeric `granted` field** (credits; always `0` until slice 11), and `remaining = max(0, allowance + granted − used)` computed on the server. The card ignores `granted` until slice 11. Put "used / remaining this period" in one server function that slice 9's balance source (F-9) can call later — **free of display rounding** — rather than inside the route |
| **SQ-28** — the route | **Replace `GET /api/business-os/usage` in place** (F-10: the card is its only consumer). **It accepts no input at all** — `range` goes, and with it the only query parameter; follow the `my-plan` precedent (F-14): no Zod schema because there is nothing to parse, and a test that the handler never reads the query or a body and ignores `?accountId=…` / `?range=…`. That test stands in for CLAUDE.md's "invalid input" case, recorded in the workplan as N/A by design. `getUser()` first, 401 without a session; the account only through `resolveAccountId(user.id)`; `export const dynamic = 'force-dynamic'`, a `Cache-Control: private, no-store` header, and the card fetches with `cache: 'no-store'` (SQ-5: consumption is never cached). Pino child logger with `correlationId`; the CLAUDE.md error format with the development guard. `range`, `breakdown`, `daily`, `calls`, `fillGaps`, `RANGE_DAYS` and `readAllowanceCredits` go. **The Layer 1.1 characterization tests are retired, not edited to pass** (F-11): delete `route.test.ts` and `route.zeroToken.test.ts` with a one-line note that slice 6 supersedes Layer 1.1 AC-23 for this route (the admin side keeps `usageSummary.test.ts`), and keep `boundaries.static.test.ts`'s "no `token_usage`, no `.rpc(`, no `.from(` in the owner route" assertions green — they still hold |

#### Other rulings

- **FR-28 allow-list (AC-21, AC-37).** The payload is small and **neutral** (no "ai" in any field name): for example `{ period: { kind: 'monthly' | 'trial_total' | 'calendar_month', resetsOn: string | null }, allowance: { amount: number, per: 'month' | 'total' } | null, used, usedByOwner, usedAutomatic, granted, remaining }`. One test asserts the **exact** set of keys, recursively; a second walks every key and fails on `/token|usd|cost|dollar|price|model|pilot|fallback/i`. The payload never echoes `user_id`. Exact values are sent (6 dp); rounding is display-only.
- **Display rounding (D-c, FR-6's documented rule).** One pure, unit-tested function (no server imports, so the card may call it) turns the exact facts into displayed figures: used as a whole number, or "less than 1" when 0 < used < 1; the two parts by the largest-remainder method so they sum to the displayed used; "left" = max(0, allowance − displayed used), where "less than 1" counts as 1, so the card never claims the full allowance is left after something ran. Full grouped numbers through `Intl.NumberFormat` for the reader's language — the "K" abbreviation goes (F-13); BA to state that in D-c.
- **"No allowance" is decided from the resolution, not from the number** (F-8). The card is ungauged when the snapshot is `unavailable`, or the resolution has an anomaly / no basis, or the value is the withheld value for that basis — never by testing `perMonth === 0` alone. A trial is recognised by the value's shape (`{ total }`), never by a cohort or tier name (the `tierLiteral` guard).
- **Entitlements registration.** Exactly one file outside the module should import from it — the server module that builds the payload — registered in `KNOWN_NON_GATE_IMPORTERS` (display only; no `check()` / `decide()`). Keep the capability id literal `'credits.allowance'` **inside** the module by exporting a small read helper from it, rather than registering the literal in `KNOWN_NON_GATES`. The route and `UsageCard.tsx` import nothing from the module. `npm run test:bos-entitlements` is a definition-of-done gate for **both** sub-slices.
- **FR-36 rate keys.** After 6a, no owner file names `monthly_ai_allowance_usd`, `pilot_credit_cost_usd`, `tokens_per_pilot_credit`, `token_usage` or "Pilot"; a source guard over the route, the new modules and the card pins it. `readTokensPerCredit` stays for Check 5. The `monthly_ai_allowance_usd` row in system config (migration `20260928`) becomes an **orphan key with no reader**; leave it (removing it is a migration for no benefit) and record it for the operator runbook / a later clean-up.
- **Tenant isolation.** The account is only ever `resolveAccountId(user.id)`; the route takes no id; every ledger read is RLS plus `.eq('user_id')`; the service-role calls are limited to those named under SQ-21. The `tenant-isolation-guard` checklist has no caller-supplied id to check, which is the point; the route test proves `?accountId=` is ignored.
- **`bos-llm-call-standards`.** Not engaged: slice 6 adds no AI call and changes no `runAiAction` behaviour. Raising the SQ-24 signal from a surface's client code touches no server AI path.
- **R-7 removal (AC-36).** The T6c list and scoping §3 item 6 are complete. Two precisions: the exit grep runs over `app lib components hooks` only (the requirement and the workplans legitimately keep the words); the `email.volume` positive control may stay, as the slice 5 code review preferred. It lands in the same release as the card (D-d) and **never before it**, because R-7's reason was that a figure should not be shown until the owner can see what has been used against it.
- **OI-10 (optional).** If done, fix it at the root — the locale threaded through `describePlanOffer` → `describePlanCapabilities` → the value wording ("per month" / "in total") — for every line, not a credits-only special case (that would be a new display exception of exactly the kind R-7's removal deletes). Locale from the invite record, validated with `isValidLocale`, falling back to the default.
- **Dead code.** `usage.last30days` and any `usage.*` key left without a consumer are removed from all three languages; the card's header comment is rewritten (it describes the $10 allowance).
- **States the scoping misses** (fold into §1's state table; not business questions — the card follows the resolver): the **trial or plan has ended** (lapsed or grace — show what the resolver resolves: in grace the allowance, once lapsed usage with no gauge); **the plan could not be read** (snapshot `unavailable`): usage with no gauge, logged at `warn`, never an error line and never "0 left"; **the owner has just moved from the trial to a paid plan**: the new period starts at zero (F-7) — true and already decided (T-6), but worth a line in KI-18.

#### The 6a / 6b split — ruling

The total (≈ 3.5–4.5 days as scoped; SA's rulings add about a quarter-day for the period module and the owner repository, and remove the per-row split sum) is over the comfort line, so a split is right. **SA re-cuts it by surface** rather than by required / optional:

| Sub-slice | Scope | Size | Gate |
|---|---|---|---|
| **6a — The dashboard card reads credits** | Scoping §3 items 1–5, 9 (card and route tests) and 10 (A-18 note); **all three refresh triggers**, including (a) and the SQ-24 signal, so AC-33 is built and QA-checked once, in one slice; the FR-36 retirement; Check 5's relabel | ≈ 3–3.5 d | AC-18, AC-21, AC-33, AC-35 (card half), AC-37 |
| **6b — The plan screen and the invite page show the credit figure** | The required R-7 removal (item 6, T6c), D-g's heading (item 8, if chosen), OI-10's localisation (item 7, optional), the entitlements and pricing doc edits | ≈ 0.75–1.25 d | AC-36 (after half); `test:bos-entitlements` |

Why: the BA's cut splits AC-33 across two slices (b and c in 6a, a in 6b), so QA cannot close it until 6b; it puts two edits to `customerPlanView.ts` and the `PlanSection` tests (the R-7 removal and the D-g heading) in different slices; and it makes 6b a mix of one required behaviour and two optional items, which is the kind of slice that gets trimmed. Cut by surface, 6a touches the dashboard, the route and the ledger reads only; 6b touches only the entitlements module and the two plan surfaces, can be built in parallel, and runs one entitlements gate. **Guardrail: 6b merges after 6a, never before** (R-7's reason). Both are held for slice 7 under D-d A; "slice 6 is not done until the R-7 rule is removed" is then met by 6b, and both sub-slices are required. If the user prefers the BA's cut, it is acceptable with one change: move trigger (a) into 6a.

**Technical note on D-d A (for the BA, not a ruling on the decision):** holding 6a, 6b and slice 7 as approved-but-unmerged PRs until slice 7 is ready means rebasing all three on `main` before the back-to-back merge, and re-running their gates after the rebase. That is manageable for about a week; it supports the BA's fall-back to C if slice 7 slips.

#### Business question for the user (beyond D-a to D-g)

- **D-h — where the one-sentence explanation of a credit appears.** D-b writes the sentence for the card. But after slice 6 the **plan screen** and the **public invite page** also show a credit figure ("Credits (32,250 per month)", "Credits (2,000 in total)") — and the invite page is the first place a **prospective customer** ever sees one (scoping §1), with nothing to say what a credit is. Options: A. card only; B. the card and the plan screen; C. the card, the plan screen and the invite page. It is a customer-facing wording choice with a trade-off (clarity for prospects versus a longer offer), so it is the user's. SA has no technical preference: B and C are each a dictionary line in three languages, in 6b. The BA may fold it into D-b as a sub-question rather than a new letter.

No other business question. D-e's and D-c's technical halves are ruled above; the remaining decisions are correctly the user's.
